# Sorted backlog (written 2026-09-06)

Working rules for any session picking an item: branch `docs/mobile-porting-checkpoint`; UAT is this VPS (`systemctl restart wealth-api` / `wealth-worker` / `wealth-frontend` after `npm run build`); production is Vercel + Railway and is NOT touched by restarts here. Never commit `backend/.env` or any key file. Fable plans, Sonnet agents edit and commit, commit trailers `Co-Authored-By: Claude <model> <noreply@anthropic.com>`. No em dashes in user-facing copy. Design changes go through Kevin as coded variants on `/design/*` before touching production components. Tick items here as they land; add the commit hash.

Context documents: `docs/compliance/finexer-agent-controls-2026-09.md` (due diligence answers, `[KEVIN]` markers), `docs/pricing/tiering-unit-economics-mcp-2026-09.md` (tier table, unit economics, scaling, MCP design), `PENNY_TOOLS.md`, `SECURITY.md`, `PRIVACY.md`, `TERMS.md`, `DEPLOY.md`.

Board: the private page /ops/go-live on UAT reads and edits these files. Sessions use `scripts/backlog.py` (`start`, `block`, `done <id> --commit <sha>`, `note`) so the file, the page and git history stay one thing. Never tick by hand.

  - note (2026-09-14, claude): Split 2026-09-14: the BOT_SECRET replacement moved to A28 and edge/transport hardening (security headers on both hosts, catch-all rate limiting, Vercel Firewall and Cloudflare decisions) is tracked as A27, since neither was a sensible unit of work inside this bundle. A26 keeps the scope document, the OAuth threat model, webhook replay and forgery tests, the secrets-in-logs audit, the design-route data check, secret rotation docs, CI dependency and container scanning, the public disclosure route and the remediation SLA.
  - note (2026-09-14, claude): Coordinator 2026-09-15: reviewed AFTER merge, because this landed in the same integrate pass as A28 before I had looked at it; integrate takes everything in review at once and I only checked the board immediately before the pass. Verdict on the production code is that it is correct and nothing needs reverting. The only functional change is in backend/app/routers/oauth.py, closing an authorisation-code and refresh-token double-redemption race; no existing check was removed, relaxed or reordered, and the only request that now fails is the second of two racing to redeem the same one-time credential, which was never a legitimate flow. The OAuth 2.1 fundamentals were already sound and are untouched: PKCE S256 only with plain refused, exact redirect_uri matching rather than prefix, constant-time verifier comparison, codes and tokens stored hashed, no logging statement anywhere in the file, and no redirect on an unknown client. scripts/session.sh gains a design-data check that runs on finish and can block it, writes nothing and costs under a second. The new frontend check fails only on a coarse whole-file regex list, so a legitimate build could trip on the word fetch inside a comment or string, worth knowing; its failure output prints only the path and a static reason, never matched content, so it cannot leak. The pentest scope document discloses ciphertext lengths and env var NAMES but no values, which is what a tester needs, and contains no credential, internal IP or host path. One follow-up raised separately: the two flagship concurrency tests cannot fail against the bug they guard.
- [x] **A27. Edge and transport hardening, none of which is covered by A26 (found 2026-09-14 by probing both live hosts): the production website sends only Strict-Transport-Security and without includeSubDomains or preload, so it has no Content-Security-Policy, no frame protection, no X-Content-Type-Options, no Referrer-Policy and no Permissions-Policy, and frontend/next.config.ts sets no headers block at all; the API on Railway sends no security headers whatsoever while serving bank data, because backend/app/main.py adds only CORS and GZip middleware; and core/ratelimit.py RULES covers just seven auth, webhook and push prefixes with no catch-all, so every data endpoint (transactions search, safe-to-spend, accounts, spend verdict, cashflow) is unlimited and is protected today only by ALLOWED_EMAILS keeping sign-up closed to ten addresses, which stops being true the moment D1 opens sign-up; add a headers block to the Next config and a security-headers middleware to FastAPI, add a sensible catch-all rate limit keyed by authenticated user rather than IP with tighter limits on expensive endpoints, and make the Redis-unavailable fallback safe on multiple replicas ahead of E5; Kevin separately owns configuring Vercel Firewall rules and deciding whether Cloudflare goes in front of the API, so record those as his decisions in a note rather than doing them** [owner: claude] [priority: p1] (done 2026-09-14, 158689b11d871f6802daf679c7b198b6a1f64ff2)

What checked out: every header is live on both hosts and correct; the catch-all degrades to a clean 429 with Retry-After and never a 500; auth, webhooks, logo and mcp paths are correctly exempted before the catch-all runs, so login, OAuth and the Stripe webhook receiver cannot be starved, which was the failure mode most worth avoiding; the Redis sliding window has a genuine in-process fallback that never raises into the request path, and with one uvicorn worker today the fallback is equally strong; the Capacitor build genuinely escapes headers() via MOBILE_EXPORT; and the reasoning for allowing unsafe-inline is accurate, since Next injects its own per-build inline flight scripts, with no analytics or error-reporting scripts present to hash instead. The new rate-limit tests are real, proved by checking out the parent commit where two files fail to import at all and four of five middleware tests fail red. Three things came out of it, each raised as its own item: the CSP blocks the Google favicon fallback so nine major UK banks silently lose their logos, which also means we were leaking bank names to Google until now; client_ip trusts X-Forwarded-For in a way that is safe behind UAT nginx but unverified through Vercel and Railway; and six expensive endpoints share one thirty-per-minute budget that ordinary browsing may exhaust. One gap left unraised: security_headers.py has no test of its own, so a future middleware-ordering change in main.py could drop every header with nothing in CI to notice.

One doc inconsistency was found and fixed on the branch as b891ae6 before merge: RELEASE.md copy-pasteable operator prompt still said to generate BOT_SECRET, six lines under prose this same branch had corrected, which mattered because that block is written to be pasted verbatim, so someone would have generated a value nothing reads and believed the bot was provisioned.

- [x] **A30. Yapily consent tokens are stored in plaintext while TrueLayer tokens are Fernet-encrypted under TOKEN_KEY (found by the A26 readiness pass, 2026-09-14, verified read-only without printing any value: TrueLayer rows carry the gAAAAA prefix, Yapily collections hold 0 live rows today but the code path is live and mounted): a Yapily consent token is functionally a bank credential and must be encrypted at rest on the same terms; note the Finexer migration intends to remove dormant Yapily entirely, so decide whether to encrypt it or remove the path, but do not leave a live code path that would write a bank credential in the clear** [owner: claude] (done 2026-09-14, 3b033725455a3f06f03a9472528bbe00f2e3d692)

The startup addition at main.py:534 is a fire-and-forget asyncio.create_task that is never awaited, so nothing inside it can propagate and it cannot stop the API booting; there is no assertion on the token key anywhere, and crypto.py already tolerates a missing key by generating and persisting one on first run, so the current healthy boot is by design rather than luck of configuration. Backward compatibility is handled properly: decrypt_token returns any value without the Fernet prefix unchanged, so a plaintext row written seconds before deploy reads fine, while a value with the right prefix under a wrong or rotated key returns None and _resolve_consent_token then fails closed and no-ops rather than syncing with garbage, which is the correct direction for a bank credential. All three write paths encrypt, routers/yapily.py:78, yapily_sync.py:43 and the defensive re-encrypt at yapily_sync.py:80, and a grep found no fourth writer. Nothing logs or returns the token: only counts are logged, and the endpoint that used to hand back the raw consent token now returns a fingerprint instead, which is a net improvement. Checked against A31, merged alongside, and the two do not interact badly, since yapily.py has no handler that interpolates exception text.

- [x] **A32. Bot credentials never expire and an unknown token leaves no audit row** [owner: claude] [priority: p2] (done 2026-09-14, b4a38002c31bcb1b32e4e972cd1b87e7d867dfc5)
  - note (2026-09-14, claude): Raised by the A28 security review 2026-09-15, flagged rather than fixed because neither is in that item literal text and A28 is the far larger win. Two gaps remain in the new bot credential system. First, there is no expires_at field at all, only manual revocation, so a minted credential lives forever unless a human remembers to revoke it; that is the same class of problem A28 was opened to fix, a long-lived credential nobody is forced to rotate, just much better scoped now. Decide whether credentials should carry an expiry by default, and if so whether expiry is enforced on every request or surfaced as a warning, since a bot whose credential silently expires mid-job is its own failure mode. Second, the audit trail only records USES OF RESOLVED CREDENTIALS: an unknown or forged token is refused but leaves no row at all, so there is nothing to detect probing against. Low urgency given 256-bit tokens make brute force infeasible, but an audit trail that cannot show you an attempt is a weaker instrument than one that can, and a pentester will say so, which is the audience A28 was written for. Consider recording refused attempts with the token hashed or truncated, never raw.
- [x] **A33. The two OAuth concurrency tests A26 added cannot fail against the vulnerability they were written to guard** [owner: claude] [priority: p2] (done 2026-09-14, 2e81fe6e89b8005fb3e780f29f6382af841e7d3b)
  - note (2026-09-14, claude): Found by a post-merge review of A26 (a663eca) on 2026-09-15. THE PRODUCTION FIX IS SOUND and needs no action: backend/app/routers/oauth.py:391-420 and :443-461 replace a find_one then update_one pair with a single conditional find_one_and_update, closing a genuine TOCTOU race where an attacker racing an intercepted authorisation code against the legitimate client could previously mint two live token pairs from one single-use credential. Mongo serialises writes to a document, so exactly one caller now wins and the loser falls through to the existing revoke-the-whole-family path, which is the right behaviour since a lost race is indistinguishable from replay. THE TESTS ARE THE PROBLEM. test_concurrent_code_exchange_only_one_winner and test_concurrent_refresh_rotation_only_one_winner in backend/tests/test_oauth_server.py both PASS against the pre-fix code, proved by swapping oauth.py for its parent commit 1d061ac and running them: 2 passed, 0 failed. The reason is that they force the interleaving with a rendezvous gate patched around find_one_and_update on the fake collection, and the pre-fix code calls update_one, so the gate never fires, the two coroutines never actually interleave, and the assertions pass for the wrong reason via the pre-existing sequential-reuse path that an older test already covers. The guard is coupled to the very call the fix introduces, so it can only ever pass. Fix by gating a seam that exists in BOTH implementations, for example triggering on the first write call whatever its name, or gating update_one as well, then PROVE it by running against 1d061ac and showing it go red. This is the fourth time on this board a test has passed for the wrong reason, after B33, B34 and B36, and the first where the test was guarding a security fix rather than a behaviour, which is why it is worth doing properly rather than deleting. Separately and not part of this item: the 234 new lines in test_truelayer_webhook.py are coverage of unchanged code rather than regression tests, which is fine but should not be cited as evidence A26 fixed anything.
- [x] **A34. Nine major UK banks lost their logos when A27's CSP landed, because the curated-bank fallback fetches favicons from Google** [owner: claude] [priority: p1] (done 2026-09-14, 3bd7063ad256f5c4dd3e6c5f9b60eef6bd336e3b)
  - note (2026-09-14, claude): Found by a post-merge review of A27 (158689b) on 2026-09-15, live on UAT now and due to reach production at the next release. A27 set img-src to self, data: and blob:, with no allowance for www.google.com. The curated bank-icon resolver falls back to the Google favicon service for any bank without a bundled local file: googleFaviconURL in components/AccountMiniCard.tsx:193-194 and 234, duplicated in HomeBrief.tsx:125, PaydayPlanCard.tsx:21, TransactionSheet.tsx:188, ReconnectStrip.tsx:58, CardTermsSheet.tsx:52 and app/planning/dismissed/bankBadge.ts:24. Of the twelve curated BANK_META entries with a domain, only five also carry a logoFile, so HSBC, Monzo, Revolut, Santander, Halifax, Nationwide, Chase, first direct and TSB all resolve to a blocked URL. It degrades to the initials chip rather than a broken image, so nobody will report it as broken, they will just quietly stop seeing bank logos across Home, Accounts, the Payday Plan, transaction detail, reconnect prompts and card terms. NOTE THE PRIVACY ANGLE BEFORE CHOOSING A FIX, because it inverts the obvious one: until A27, every user browser was telling Google which banks that person holds, on every page that rendered an account. The CSP has stopped that. So re-allowing www.google.com in img-src would restore the logos by restoring a data leak to a third party, which sits badly with a product whose whole pitch is being trusted with someone finances. The better fixes are to route these through the app own same-origin /logo/ proxy, which already exists and is what the other logo path uses, or to bundle local files for the nine affected banks. Recommend the proxy, since it fixes every future bank too rather than nine. Also worth folding in: that googleFaviconURL helper is duplicated across seven files, the same copy-in-two-places pattern that keeps costing this board, so whatever the fix, it should end up in one place.
  - note (2026-09-14, claude): Coordinator 2026-09-16: reviewed AFTER merge, since this went through an integrate pass I did not run. The important thing is right: the CSP was NOT widened, confirmed live as img-src self, data: and blob: unchanged, and the browser-side Google favicon call is gone from the code entirely rather than being allowed back in. The privacy reasoning is written into the code comment where the next person will find it. Consolidation was done properly too, eleven files down to one bankLogoSrc with ninety lines deleted against sixty-one added, rather than eleven hand-matched copies, which is the pattern that has cost this board repeatedly. The one thing to know is that this closes the leak without restoring the logos: the nine banks without a bundled asset now render the initials chip permanently. That is a defensible choice and the comment argues it, but it is not the only one available, because the same-origin /logo/ proxy at backend/app/routers/logos.py:94 already fetches these server-side and was verified returning real PNGs for HSBC, Monzo and Revolut. Raised separately rather than as a defect here.
- [ ] **A35. A27's IP rate limit trusts X-Forwarded-For, which is safe on UAT's nginx but unverified through Vercel and Railway** [owner: kevin] [priority: p2]
  - note (2026-09-14, claude): Found by the A27 post-merge review 2026-09-15. client_ip() in backend/app/core/ratelimit.py:93-108 reads X-Real-IP first, then the first entry of X-Forwarded-For, then the socket peer, with no trusted-hop count. On UAT that is safe and was verified live: the nginx site config sets X-Real-IP to remote_addr unconditionally, and nginx overwrites rather than appends, so a client-forged header cannot win. Production is a different topology and could not be verified from here without hitting it: Vercel proxies at its edge to Railway, and Next own proxy path sets x-forwarded-for only if absent, meaning it preserves a client-supplied value rather than overwriting it. If either hop passes attacker input through, the anonymous-caller IP limit that A27 exists to add is trivially bypassed in production while looking solid on UAT. Impact is bounded, an abuse throttle bypassed rather than any data exposure, which is why this is p2 rather than p1. Kevin item because answering it means checking Vercel and Railway behaviour or running a controlled test against production, neither of which a session should do unprompted. If it turns out a hop is untrusted, the fix is to count trusted hops from the right rather than taking the leftmost entry.
- [x] **A36. Six expensive endpoints share one 30-per-minute budget, which ordinary browsing may exhaust** [owner: claude] [priority: p2] (done 2026-09-15, a08250ca9ac3375bb3ef5638e5ab9c6089d4beee)
  - note (2026-09-14, claude): Found by the A27 post-merge review 2026-09-15. EXPENSIVE_PREFIXES in backend/app/core/ratelimit.py:86-89 covers /transactions/search, /safe-to-spend, /cashflow, /spend/verdict, /money-shape and /savings-insights, and all six share ONE bucket of 30 requests per 60 seconds keyed on the user, not one bucket each. Those six cover nearly every substantive screen: Home Safe-to-Spend, the Spend verdict, the money shape, Upcoming and Planning cashflow, savings-insight callouts and transaction search. One call site actively works against the budget: fetchVerdictData in frontend/lib/verdictCache.ts:24-32 fires a real network call on every invocation unless one is already in flight, deliberately, so a stale verdict never survives a paint, and it is called independently from HomePage.tsx:404 on every Home mount, SpendTrends.tsx:1425 and SpendPage.tsx:360. So browsing Home to Spend to Shape to Upcoming a few times in a minute, or opening a couple of tips, or running one search, can consume a large share of thirty. The suite does not catch this: test_auth_middleware_catch_all.py:110-127 models four Home reloads at three expensive hits each, twelve in total, and never models moving between screens. Nobody has hit it yet as far as anyone knows, and the failure is a clean 429 with Retry-After rather than an error, but a legitimate user being throttled for using the app is worse than the abuse it prevents. Fix by splitting the budget per prefix, raising it, or making the verdict cache check itself before firing; and extend the realistic-load test to walk between screens rather than reloading one.
  - note (2026-09-15, claude): Coordinator 2026-09-16: independently reviewed, SHIP. The fix is the per-prefix split rather than a raised limit: the key changed from one shared catchall-user-expensive bucket to one per prefix per user, with the numbers unchanged at 30 per 60 seconds. The gap this item was actually about is closed: new tests walk Home to Spend to Upcoming to Planning and back using request paths read off the real frontend call graph rather than guessed, and the old code genuinely throttles that walk on lap three at /cashflow, which is the bug. Four new tests fail red against main. ACCEPTED TRADE-OFF WORTH NAMING: a caller rotating across all six expensive prefixes can now draw up to 180 expensive requests a minute from one identity before any 429, against 30 under the shared bucket. Single-endpoint hammering is bounded exactly as before at 30, and the unchanged per-user 300 and per-IP 600 catch-alls still bound the total, but the ceiling for spread-out abuse is six times higher. That is the intended shape of the fix rather than an oversight, and it is the price of not throttling ordinary browsing.
- [ ] **A37. A31's exception-leak guard misses any handler that stores the exception in a variable first** [owner: claude]
  - note (2026-09-14, claude): Found by a post-merge review of A31 (0ad6138) on 2026-09-15. The fix itself is complete and needs no action: all roughly thirty sites across penny_tools.py, can_i.py, transactions.py and pdf.py now log with exc_info and return a generic calm message, and nothing was found still interpolating a caught exception into a user-visible string. The guard is the gap. backend/tests/test_no_raw_exception_leak.py walks every except-as block via AST and flags a return or raise whose subtree contains the bound exception name. The reviewer proved it genuinely fails red against the pre-fix code, correctly listing all forty-one old leaky sites, so it is a real regression test and not decoration. But it was also shown to MISS one hop of indirection: a handler written as except Exception as e, then msg = str(e), then return an error containing msg, passes the scan, while the same-statement version is caught. The scan inspects only the AST of the return expression, never the dataflow of the bound name through earlier statements. That is the same weakness as G62, a test carried by matching source shape rather than exercising behaviour, just implemented with ast rather than regex, and the commit message calling it AST-based oversells it. Storing an exception in a variable before returning is an ordinary thing to write, so this will eventually let one through. Either extend the scan to follow an assignment whose right-hand side embeds the bound name, and then check downstream returns for that assigned name, or change the docstring to state plainly that only zero-hop leaks are caught, so nobody trusts it further than it deserves. Prefer the first.
- [ ] **A38. Bundle real bank logos from a licensed source, replacing the brand-coloured initials chips A34 left in place (2026-09-15): A34 removed the Google favicon fallback because it disclosed which banks a user holds to a third party on every page load, and fell back to BankBadge initials for the eleven affected institutions (HSBC, Monzo, Revolut, Santander, Halifax, Nationwide, Chase, first direct, TSB, plus MONO and MPESA), four of which Kevin himself holds; the A34 agent tried scraping each bank's own apple-touch-icon and got 404s, an HTML redirect page mislabelled as a PNG, and inconsistent sizes, so it deliberately shipped no scraped assets; the backend already has Logo.dev wired with a LOGODEV_TOKEN slot, so use that or each bank's official brand kit; note the licensing position it established: nominative fair use covers the trademark question under TMA 1994 s.11(2) and is standard fintech practice, but copyright is separate, simple wordmarks are likely below the originality threshold while graphic marks like the HSBC hexagon and Nationwide arrow are not, and Wikipedia fair-use tags do not transfer to commercial use; the five already-bundled logos carry no provenance documentation, so add a NOTICE file recording the source and basis for every logo shipped** [owner: claude] [state: blocked: superseded by G107, same work with a sharper brief]
  - note (2026-09-16, claude): Superseded by G107, which covers the same work with a sharper brief: it names the 22 unbundled providers, restates the privacy constraint from A34 and A27 that must hold (no CSP widening, no third-party favicon fetch), and adds the test that would have caught this, a check failing when a BANK_META entry claims a logoFile that is not on disk. Working G107 instead.
- [ ] **A39. The nine missing bank logos can be restored through the same-origin proxy we already have, with no privacy cost** [owner: claude] [priority: p2]
  - note (2026-09-14, claude): Raised by a post-merge review of A34 (3bd7063) on 2026-09-16. A34 IS CORRECT AND SHOULD NOT BE REVERTED: it left the CSP alone, removed the browser-side Google favicon call entirely, and consolidated a helper that had been duplicated across eleven files into one bankLogoSrc, which is exactly the right shape and closed a real leak, since that request told Google from the user own browser and IP which banks that person holds, on every page that rendered an account. What it did NOT do is restore the logos. bankLogoSrc now returns a local file or null, so the nine curated banks without a bundled asset render the initials chip permanently: HSBC, Monzo, Revolut, Santander, Halifax, Nationwide, Chase, first direct and TSB. Only five bundled files exist in frontend/public/banks, amex, barclays, lloyds, natwest and starling. THE LOGOS ARE RECOVERABLE WITH NO PRIVACY COST and the route already exists. backend/app/routers/logos.py:94 serves GET /logo/{domain}, trying Logo.dev first and falling back to Google favicons SERVER-SIDE, and frontend/lib/api.ts already exports logoUrl for it. Because the server makes that request, Google sees the server address rather than the user, cannot tie it to an individual, and the response is same-origin so the CSP is satisfied unchanged. Verified against the live UAT API on 2026-09-16: /logo/hsbc.co.uk returned 200 with a 21894 byte PNG, /logo/monzo.com 200 with 9953 bytes, /logo/revolut.com 200 with 6857 bytes. So the fix is to have bankLogoSrc fall back to logoUrl(meta.domain) when there is no bundled file, which restores all nine and every future curated bank, rather than bundling nine more assets by hand. Check the proxy caching and its 404 path first, since a bank the upstreams miss should still land on the initials chip rather than a broken image, and confirm the existing onError fallback in BankBadge still covers that. Kevin call if he would rather keep initials as a deliberate look, but he should know the choice is available and costs nothing in privacy.
- [ ] **A40. A31's no-raw-exception-leak guard keys its allowlist by line number, so any edit that shifts lines in an allowlisted file breaks an unrelated branch: it has already broken twice in two days, on B31 (two billing.py entries shifted) and F18 (fifteen entries across mcp.py and penny_tools.py), each time costing an agent a debugging detour and each time fixed by renumbering rather than by anything meaningful; re-key the allowlist to something stable such as the enclosing function name plus the exception variable, or a marker comment on the line itself, so the guard keeps catching genuinely new leaks without failing on unrelated edits; the guard itself is sound and has already caught a real new leak in B37, so keep its behaviour and change only how entries are identified** [owner: claude]
- [x] **A41. Internal pentest methodology, Claude's independent draft: research and write docs/security/pentest-methodology-claude.md against the surfaces already enumerated in docs/security/pentest-scope-2026-09.md rather than re-enumerating them, naming the testing standard being followed (for example OWASP WSTG, ASVS, the API Security Top 10, and MASVS for the two Capacitor shells), a concrete per-surface test case list with the expected result for each case, the tooling and how it is run from this VPS inside the repo scope restriction, a severity rubric, the evidence format every executed test must record, and an execution plan split into work packages sized to become individual board items; this item plans only and executes no testing whatsoever, and must be written without reading the Codex draft from the sibling item so the two are genuinely independent** [owner: claude] [priority: p1] [unblocks: Q11] (done 2026-09-18, 9284970)

HIGHEST: line 297 calls this "a CREST-accredited external engagement", echoed at lines 85 and 137, which directly contradicts A41's own title, Internal pentest methodology, and A45's explicit instruction never to use wording that could be read as an independent third-party engagement; A7 remains the separate external CREST item.   - note (2026-09-16, claude): Raised by the G107 review 2026-09-18. NOT A BLOCKER ON THAT MERGE and not a criticism of the work: frontend/public/banks/NOTICE.md is an unusually careful licensing note. It separates trademark from copyright, states a legal theory rather than assuming a licence, says explicitly that Logo.dev own terms grant no rights over the underlying marks, discloses Logo.dev as a data processor consistent with PRIVACY.md:95, and gives a per-file provenance table with source domain and fetch date while honestly recording that the five PRE-EXISTING logos have no known origin rather than inventing one. The byte and dimension figures in it were independently verified against the committed blobs and match exactly. TWO THINGS A LAWYER SHOULD SEE. First, the provenance table is a hand-typed assertion, not reproducible evidence: there is no committed fetch script, response log or hash tying each shipped PNG to a specific upstream response, so an auditor cannot re-derive from the repository alone that a given file came from a given call on a given date; it rests on a self-report. Second, the note reasons in terms of NOMINATIVE FAIR USE, which is a United States doctrine, while this is a UK product heading for a UK store submission, where the operative concept is honest or referential use under section 11 of the Trade Marks Act 1994, mirroring Article 14 of the EU Trade Mark Directive. Related in spirit, not identical in test. The underlying position looks sound and matches what Plaid, TrueLaye...

That breaks the redaction rule this programme runs under, PENTEST-METHODOLOGY section 7.4 and row 11, which requires any captured secret to be masked to first and last four characters or replaced with a SHA-256 fingerprint, and it is the exact class of value scripts/pentest_redact.py was shipped under A47 to handle. Fix: pipe it through that tool, mask mode for a recognisable prefix or fingerprint mode if any doubt, and keep the raw manifest only under the gitignored .pentest-evidence/A54-2026-09-20/ directory. Context, not an excuse: the same key is already in git history via 3cc286b0 and a082bf7c, which is what H1 records and why Kevin chose to restrict the key in Google Cloud rather than rewrite history. That makes the marginal exposure small, but re-committing it into the working tree, in the directory reserved for SANITISED records, is still wrong and would be a poor look in a security evidence document. Everything else on this branch can stand.
  - note (2026-09-21, claude): Merged 2026-09-21 after the redaction fix, which I verified: the live Google API key is now masked as AIza...BzY8, the exact first-four-last-four shape scripts/pentest_redact.py mask produces, and a sweep of every file on the branch found zero secret-shaped material. One minor inaccuracy merged knowingly rather than bounced for a second round, since it is a single word in a descriptive sentence and not a verdict or a finding. The replacement text calls the values 'derived from the committed, intentionally-non-secret capacitor-spike/google-services.json'. That file is NOT committed: it is gitignored and untracked, confirmed with git ls-files and git check-ignore on main, and has been since 2026-09-05, which is exactly what H1 records. It WAS tracked until then and an old copy remains in git history, which is the nuance H1 exists to hold. The 'intentionally-non-secret' half is fair, since an Android API key ships inside every APK and is restricted by package name, which is why Kevin chose restriction over rotation. Worth correcting the tense next time this file is touched so a later reader does not conclude the config is still tracked.

UAT's Redis (the openclaw_redis container that wealth-api uses) is reachable over public IPv6: docker-proxy publishes 6379 on [::] with no IPv6 DNAT, so inbound v6 to the host's global address terminates on the host socket through ip6tables INPUT (policy ACCEPT) and the DOCKER-USER DROP in redis-firewall.service never applies; IPv4 is protected by the DNAT path; the container's --requirepass value looks like a placeholder; and redis-firewall.service is enabled but inactive so even the v4 rule may not survive a reboot. Fix by publishing the port on loopback only, or adding INPUT drops for 6379 on eth0 for both v4 and v6 to the unit and making it actually run, and set a real password** [owner: kevin] [priority: p1]
  - note (2026-09-20, claude): Found 2026-09-20 while reviewing A65, from read-only host inspection, no external probe made. Evidence: docker port openclaw_redis shows 6379/tcp on 0.0.0.0 and [::]; ss -ltnp shows docker-proxy owns both listeners; ip6tables -t nat -L DOCKER has no 6379 DNAT; ip6tables INPUT policy ACCEPT with no 6379 rule; ufw inactive; host has global IPv6 2a02:c207:2306:9477::1; redis-firewall.service is enabled but is-active reports inactive. Reachability from outside is inferred from the packet path, not confirmed. Redis holds UAT rate-limit state and response cache, so write access would let an outsider tamper with limits or poison cached responses for the ten UAT testers; production uses the Railway Redis plugin and is unaffected. Cheapest fix that also covers reboot: recreate the container with -p 127.0.0.1:6379:6379 (it is another project's container, so Kevin decides), or add ExecStart lines to redis-firewall.service for iptables and ip6tables -I INPUT -i eth0 -p tcp --dport 6379 -j DROP and systemctl start it. Either way replace the placeholder password and update REDIS_URL in backend/.env, then restart wealth-api and wealth-worker.

- [x] **A84. Deleted account's session token is not invalidated, still authenticates for up to 7 days (API-15 finding)** [owner: claude] [priority: p2] (done 2026-09-22, 40fed3918349efa26faceff3fc611f2f1f074c64)
  - note (2026-09-20, claude): Validated live during A50's API-15 pass (2026-09-20). After DELETE /account succeeded (confirm=DELETE) against the account this pass actually reached, the identical, unchanged bearer token was replayed against GET /profile, GET /accounts, GET /connections, GET /commitments and GET /checkpoints: every one returned 200 (empty/default bodies matching the now-erased state), none returned 401/403. backend/app/core/auth.py::current_user only validates the itsdangerous signature and SESSION_MAX_AGE (7 days, backend/app/core/config.py); there is no server-side session store or revocation list, so a session token remains a valid credential for up to 7 days after the account it names, and all its data, have been permanently erased. By code reading alone (not tested further, to avoid creating new data under a deleted identity), PUT /profile's upsert=True write path (backend/app/routers/profile.py::update_profile) would very likely let the same token recreate a fresh user_profiles document under the deleted email, since nothing on that route checks the account still exists. This is exactly the candidate expectation PENTEST-METHODOLOGY.md named for API-15 (a stateless signature with no server-side revocation may still authenticate after deletion), now confirmed against the expected secure result: a deleted account's session should not continue to work. Suggested severity for Kevin/ISM to confirm: Medium(P3), a contained session-management gap (no new data exposure, since the underlying...
  - note (2026-09-20, claude): Escalation evidence 2026-09-20: A84 is worse than a stale read. During the voided A51 run (A78), PT-A's account was deleted while its session token stayed valid; the token was then used to UPLOAD data, which persisted keyed to the identity's email. After PT-A was recreated with the same email, that data reattached to the fresh account: a 'PENTEST CANARY BANK' statement account (~£987.66) is present on the recreated PT-A now, created by a token whose account had already been deleted. So the missing server-side revocation lets a deleted-account token WRITE persistent state, not just read, and because records key by email that state survives account recreation. This raises A84's impact: for a real user, a leaked/retained token after account deletion can keep writing for up to SESSION_MAX_AGE (7 days). Recommend A84 be re-triaged with this write-impact in mind. The leftover canary account needs deleting by the app owner via the app; an agent DELETE was blocked by the session permission classifier.
  - note (2026-09-21, claude): Triage 2026-09-21 confirms HIGH (P2), formalising the earlier escalation note over the initial Medium suggestion: a deleted account's session token stays valid up to SESSION_MAX_AGE (~7 days) and can WRITE persistent data that reattaches on account recreation (live-observed), a data-erasure/GDPR-adjacent failure, not a stale-read-only gap.
  - note (2026-09-22, claude): Rejection detail (the reject reason field truncates at 200 chars). Verified on the branch: is_revoked is called 3x in core/auth.py, 2x in routers/auth.py, 0x in routers/mcp.py and routers/oauth.py. resolve_mcp_principal (mcp.py ~312-333) validates a sorted_at_ token only against oauth_tokens_col revoked_at/expires_at, never checking the account still exists. _handle_refresh_token_grant (oauth.py ~423-461) mints a fresh pair from doc['uid'] with no account-existence check, and each redemption resets the 30-day TTL, so a refreshing MCP client keeps authenticating as a deleted uid indefinitely, well past the item's 'up to 7 days'. erase_user cannot clean these up either: it deletes on {user_id: uid} or {_id: uid}, but oauth token docs are _id=token_hash with the user in a 'uid' field, so neither clause matches. Given A84's own notes record live WRITE impact (canary account reattaching after recreation), the same write path is reachable with an OAuth token. Fix on this same branch, same root cause, not a new item: delete/revoke oauth_tokens_col rows matching {uid: uid} in erase_user, and/or have resolve_mcp_principal and _handle_refresh_token_grant consult is_revoked plus account existence; add a regression test mirroring test_old_token_rejected_after_delete_account_before_any_profile_write for a sorted_at_/sorted_rt_ pair. Rest of the branch is sound and should be kept: 2794 passed, both session-token paths fail closed on a tombstone-lookup error.

PATCH status=done on the first -> 200. DELETE (cancel) on that same done commitment -> 200, status flips straight to cancelled with no transition guard. So one PATCH /commitments/{id} with body {'status': 'done', 'contribute_delta': 100} against an active commitment passes the transition guard (active->done is allowed) AND the contribute guard (current_status is still 'active'), returns 200, and lands both atomically: status done with contributed 100.0. That is exactly the contribution-on-a-terminal-commitment bug the item targets, just reached in one call instead of two. frontend/lib/api.ts:2540-2549 permits sending both fields together and nothing server-side stops a raw client. None of the 15 new tests exercise a combined body. Fix on this same branch: check contribute_delta against updates.get('status', current_status), or apply both guards to the post-update state, and add a combined-body regr...

MCP-06 (A53 pentest, run A53-2026-09-21): app/services/mcp_mask.py masks MCP tool output only structurally (drops keys/whole transaction-shaped rows by shape), never sanitises the CONTENT of a merchant name, recurring-series description, or insight trigger string it keeps; an instruction-shaped string in any such field (provider-supplied text a user does not fully control) reaches the connecting external assistant (Claude.ai, ChatGPT) over /mcp completely unmodified, a live LLM01:2025 prompt-injection surface with no mitigation; recorded Fail, genuinely open per the methodology's own MCP-06 row; test-only, pinned by backend/tests/test_mcp_endpoint.py::test_mask_does_not_sanitise_instruction_shaped_text_in_merchant_or_category_fields** [owner: claude] [priority: p2] [unblocks: Q11] (done 2026-09-22, 9c5b7ef9a8126dd3e5a4076c6207b30a14690bbe)
  - note (2026-09-21, claude): Triage 2026-09-21 (same-model independent validation): CONFIRMED and rated HIGH (P2), up from the p3 it was raised at. mcp_mask.py masks only by key-name and structural shape; it never sanitises the CONTENT of a kept string, so attacker-influenceable text (a payment reference or transaction description a victim's bank later surfaces, attacker bar as low as sending the victim money with a crafted reference) reaches the connecting agentic assistant unsanitised. The only named defence, MCP_SERVER_INSTRUCTIONS, is advisory to the connecting model, which the methodology explicitly says not to trust as a control, so the technical control is absent, not partial (contrast A79, a bounded non-agentic OpenRouter call). MITIGATING: the MCP connector is off in production by design (A17), so live exposure is UAT-only today; this is a must-fix BEFORE the connector is enabled in production (F2), not an actively-exploitable-in-prod issue now.

- [x] **A92. Fix production rate-limit IP-spoofing bypass: client_ip() in backend/app/core/ratelimit.py trusts a caller-supplied X-Real-IP/X-Forwarded-For verbatim, and production's Vercel/Railway proxy chain does not strip or overwrite it (unlike UAT's nginx), so every IP-keyed rate limit is bypassable by rotating the header; found and live-confirmed during A49/WP2 (API-12), proposed severity High (P2), Kevin's call per SECURITY.md** [owner: claude] [priority: p2] (done 2026-09-22, d5fbdfe1ecbef3049cad48752097e2400a6f26e9)
  - note (2026-09-21, claude): Triage 2026-09-21: CONFIRMED but rated MEDIUM (P3), NOT the High it was proposed at. client_ip() (ratelimit.py:107-114) trusts X-Real-IP/X-Forwarded-For verbatim, so production IP-keyed limits are header-spoofable (UAT's nginx strips them). But there is no password login to brute-force (Google/Apple id-token sign-in only), and the per-user limits that bound the data-bearing routes key on the validated identity, not the IP, so they are untouched. What is removed is cost/volume control on unauthenticated, non-data operations. Real, fix soon (trust only the platform-injected header), but not a data-exposure High.
  - note (2026-09-22, claude): Deploy gate (review 2026-09-22): with the default TRUSTED_PROXY_HOPS=0 every client behind a proxy collapses to one rate-limit bucket. UAT: TRUSTED_PROXY_HOPS=1 added to backend/.env on 2026-09-22 (nginx appends via proxy_add_x_forwarded_for, verified). Production: do NOT release this to Railway until the hop count behind Vercel plus Railway is verified by logging one real request's X-Forwarded-For; set the variable to the observed count, never higher (too high reopens the spoof, too low only coarsens buckets). Until set, production would share one 600/60 catch-all and one 30/60 /auth bucket across all users, which is a login lockout risk. Add to the release checklist.

- [ ] **A99. Account deletion will stop erasing the Mono and M-Pesa collections once A98 lands, because services/retention.py erase_user sweeps dynamically. Verified 2026-09-21: erase_user (line 60) iterates dir(app.db.collections) and deletes from every attribute whose name ends _col, matching on user_id or a uid-keyed _id. A98 removes the mono_connections, mono_accounts, mono_transactions, mpesa_accounts and mpesa_transactions handles from that module, so the sweep no longer sees those collections at all, and account_has_data reads a name list with the same consequence. The routine's own docstring promises to erase every trace of a uid, and routers/profile.py delete_account delegates to it, as does the dormant-user sweep behind SECURITY.md section 6, so this is a data-retention and GDPR exposure rather than a tidiness issue. All five collections hold zero documents on UAT, so nothing is at risk there. Production is Atlas and was not checked from this session, so the actual exposure is unknown and must be established before or alongside A98 merging. Decide and act: if production holds documents in any of the five, either drop those collections deliberately as a data decision of Kevin's, or keep just enough of a name list in retention.py to keep erasing them. If production holds none, record that finding so the absence is evidenced rather than assumed. Note the same class of bug will recur for any future collection removal, so consider whether erase_user should assert against an explicit manifest rather than trusting dir().** [owner: claude]

The data is otherwise inert, since no code reads these collections after A98, and the blast radius is two test documents rather than a real customer, which is why A98 was merged rather than rejected. Decide between two fixes: drop the orphaned production documents outright, which is the cleaner end state given the region is gone, or keep a *_col binding purely so erase_user still sweeps them. Note the same trap applies to any future collection removal, so the durable fix may be a guard asserting that every collection holding a user_id has a *_col binding.** [owner: claude] [priority: p2]

Native bank-connect round trip is three separate faults that together make the first-run experience feel broken (Kevin 2026-09-22 on the production app): first, components/BankPickerSheet.tsx line 82 launches Finexer consent with window.location.href = auth_url, which in the Capacitor shell hands the navigation to the system browser, so the user is dumped into full Chrome, while login already does this correctly via Browser.open in lib/nativeAuth.ts line 250, the in-app browser view that keeps the app framing and the system-browser security context banks expect, so switch consent to the same mechanism and close it on return; do not embed consent in an in-app WebView, open banking providers and most banks block that context, the Custom Tab or SFSafariViewController is the right answer; second, the callback page in routers/finexer.py lines 141 to 176 is web-centric, it shows Continue which sends to /accounts on the web product and Return to app which fires wealthdash://auth-complete, with a 1.8 second auto-attempt and a fallback to /accounts, and it cannot tell it is in the native flow, so a native user sees a confusing choice where one option leaves the app for the store shell; carry a native marker through the state parameter so the page auto-returns with a single fallback button, and on the web send to /accounts?syncing=1 because app/components/AccountsPage.tsx lines 733 to 745 already has a 3 second poll for that flag which the callback never triggers today; third and worst, nothing in the app handles the bank-connect deep link at all, the only appUrlOpen listeners live inside the login promise in nativeAuth.ts lines 305 to 320 and are torn down when login settles, so wealthdash://auth-complete brings the app forward and no code reacts, which is why the picker sheet stayed open with its spinner, Home still showed Connect your first bank while Recent transactions had populated, and the estate and Safe to Spend card appeared only when something else happened to refetch; add a global deep-link handler registered once at app start that closes the picker, clears every client cache via the invalidate-all that G138 builds, navigates to a syncing state and polls until accounts land, and replace the stale-component window with a proper loading state, since half-rendered Home with a fresh-user card above real transactions reads as a bug; No server-side backstop on the native purchase gate (found by the post-merge review of B26, 2026-09-14): routers/billing.py's POST /billing/checkout and POST /billing/portal have zero platform awareness, so the entire native gate is client-side in frontend/lib/nativeAuth.ts's canPurchaseInApp; once BILLING_ENABLED goes true a regression, a stale cached bundle or a webview edge case would silently reopen the hole B26 exists to close, with nothing server-side to stop it and no logging to notice; Apple and Google review the client UI rather than raw API calls so this is defence in depth rather than a live risk, but add a client-platform signal checked server-side, or at minimum an alert when a checkout session is created from a request that looks native, before go-live. Related: canPurchaseInApp resolves to web during server rendering since Capacitor's bridge is absent in the Node build, which is not exploitable today only because every purchase surface waits on an async billing_live fetch before showing a button; a future component that reads it at first paint would flash a real button for one frame on native, so add a warning comment or a hook that only resolves after mount** [owner: claude] [priority: p2] (done 2026-09-14, 5a9b421204f9c6c535c39779b3d0718e6ada3281)

- [x] **B34. Backend tests assert on ambient Stripe configuration, so B25's sandbox bring-up broke every merge on the board** [owner: claude] [priority: p1] (done 2026-09-13, 9a0f444422b6222e04a1d0b81dfb1d12534f6a53)
  - note (2026-09-13, claude): Coordinator 2026-09-14: merged as 9a0f444. Worth recording why this existed, because the same shape will recur: the test was not wrong when it was written, it simply asserted billing_live is False without pinning the flag, and passed for months only because no machine running it had Stripe configured. B25 configuring the sandbox made it fail, and because integrate runs the whole suite before merging, one ambient assertion blocked every item on the board, B33 and G57 included, neither of which touched billing. The helper now lives in tests/conftest.py so the correct pattern is reachable from any test file. The sweep found no other unpatched config-derived flag.

That is what the standing rule said must not happen until B29 lands, and B29 is still blocked on Kevin approving one sentence of wording. Two things make this less alarming than it sounds: the key is test mode, so no real money can move, and B26 gates the native purchase surfaces client-side. But B31, the server-side backstop, is still open and unstarted, so the native gate is client-side only while the flag is live. Nothing has been changed: the environment was left exactly as B25 set it, because turning another session off mid-bring-up would be worse than naming the situation. Kevin decides: either accept a test-mode flag on UAT as the point of B25 and note that here, or ask for it to be turned off until B29 and B31 land.
- [x] **B36. Two entitlement bugs in _handle_subscription_upsert found by B25's live Stripe matrix on 2026-09-13: status_map has no 'incomplete' (nor 'paused') and defaults unknown statuses to 'active', so a subscriber who abandons the 3D Secure challenge and never pays is granted the paid tier; and current_period_end moved to the subscription item in Stripe API version 2026-08-26.dahlia so expires_at is never written, meaning a past_due subscriber's dunning grace never ends and a cancel-at-period-end never takes effect; make the status mapping explicit and fail closed, read the period end from the item with a subscription-level fallback, and consider pinning the API version so a field cannot silently move again** [owner: claude] [priority: p1] (done 2026-09-13, d8a68a6af0918024b9c10c57c733a19f6557a69f)
  - note (2026-09-13, claude): Fixed the test defect flagged in review: test_past_due_subscriber_keeps_tier_until_expires_at_then_loses_it manually overwrote expires_at without ever checking the value the code computed, so it passed against pre-fix billing.py too. Chose the assert-before-mutating approach (kept alongside its sibling tests style) over monkeypatching datetime.now: added an assertion right after handle_event that fake_subs.docs[0]["expires_at"] equals the items[0].current_period_end timestamp, before the existing manual overwrite that simulates time passing. Verified in /tmp scratch worktree (removed after): RED against mains pre-fix billing.py -> AssertionError: assert None is not None at the new assert stored_expires_at is not None line; GREEN against the branchs own billing.py -> 1 passed. Docstring corrected to describe what the test verifies. Full backend suite from the worktree: 2350 passed. Committed 9d2d425 and pushed to feature-B36-billing-entitlement-bugs. Item left in review, no state change.
  - note (2026-09-13, claude): Coordinator 2026-09-14: independently reviewed and returned SHIP on the production code, with one test defect found and since fixed on the branch as 9d2d425. What the reviewer confirmed: every one of Stripe eight documented subscription statuses is mapped explicitly and the default changed from active to expired, so an unrecognised future status now withholds entitlement instead of granting it, which was the actual bug. Equally important, it does not cut off anyone who has paid: past_due and unpaid are unchanged and still hold the paid tier until expires_at passes, so an ordinary card retry does not strip access, and the two statuses newly moved to expired, incomplete and paused, both describe subscriptions that never had a successful payment. Three of the five new tests were proved genuine by running them against the pre-fix code, where they fail red. The defect was the fourth: test_past_due_subscriber_keeps_tier_until_expires_at_then_loses_it overwrote the expires_at value it was meant to be testing, so it passed against the broken code too, the same shape as B33 and B34. Now fixed by asserting the stored expires_at matches the item timestamp before the overwrite, verified red against pre-fix code with "assert None is not None" and green on the branch, full suite 2350 passed. No conflict with B33 to_dict or B34 conftest helper, and the pinned STRIPE_API_VERSION is in scope per the item text.
- [x] **B37. Penny loop retries transient provider errors and stops reporting an infrastructure failure as out of scope** [owner: claude] [priority: p1] (done 2026-09-14, f99397c28940fc0d40a31108d3b1919c37c8b3ff)
  - note (2026-09-14, claude): Found in a 2026-09-14 audit of Penny's harness against LangChain's custom-agent-harness checklist. app/services/penny_agent.py returns None on any OpenRouter non-200 (no retry at all), and app/routers/can_i.py line 349 turns that None into the deterministic refusal with out_of_scope true, so a 429 or a 502 tells the user their answerable question was outside what Penny can work out. The comment at can_i.py line 365 records this firing twice on genuinely answerable questions on 2026-08-31, where the fix applied was to reword the refusal rather than separate the causes. Two changes wanted: one bounded retry on 429 and 5xx inside the existing wall-clock budget, and a distinct failure reply with out_of_scope false so infrastructure never speaks as scope.
- [ ] **B38. Persist Penny tool-selection traces and add a golden-question eval gate** [owner: claude] [priority: p2]
  - note (2026-09-14, claude): Found in the same 2026-09-14 harness audit. The loop logs tools used and rounds at INFO and meters cost per round to llm_usage_col, but nothing persists which tools were chosen for which question shape and nothing can replay a question. The routing bug that caused the whole loop-first rebuild (PENNY_TOOLS.md) has no regression net. Cheapest useful version: record the tool sequence on the existing usage doc, plus a golden set of roughly 30 questions drawn from docs/penny/question-inventory run against a fake model in CI, asserting tool selection rather than phrasing.
- [x] **B39. Penny knows which screen you are on but not what it is showing, so a question about the page in front of you gets answered from raw tools instead of the view (Kevin 2026-09-14): PennyAskContext in components/PennySheetProvider.tsx carries only a route name from a closed set of ten plus an optional summary string that its own comment calls decorative context, not required; exactly one surface in the whole app supplies that summary (app/spend/shape/ShapePage.tsx line 78) and PennyConversation.tsx gates it with summaryConsumedRef to the FIRST request per screen, so even on that one page a follow-up question arrives with no grounding at all; give every screen a structured, deterministic context object describing what is actually rendered, the route, the active period or filter or scope, the headline figures with the labels the user can see, and the ids of the visible cards, computed by the same code that renders them so the two can never disagree; carry it for the whole conversation from that screen rather than one shot, refresh it when the view changes, and pass it to the model as facts it may phrase but never derive from, per the engine doctrine; it must respect the no-app-artifacts rule so Penny explains or adds rather than reading the screen back, and it must carry no account numbers, sort codes or provider ids since it enters the prompt; add per-screen tests asserting the context matches the rendered figures, and start with the screens where the mismatch bites most, Home's Safe-to-Spend, Spend's period view and Upcoming** [owner: claude] [priority: p2] (done 2026-09-15, 5f4635b550ded1fb400cce9cb7060ab12131e4ec)
  - note (2026-09-15, claude): Coordinator 2026-09-16: independently reviewed, SHIP, with the two questions that mattered most answered properly. WHAT LEAVES THE DEVICE is a fixed allow-list, not a data dump: route, scope, verdict, an as-of stamp and at most six figures whose values are PRE-FORMATTED STRINGS such as "£83" rather than raw numbers, capped on both client and server, so the shape itself has no field a transaction list or an account number could occupy. The real trust boundary is server-side in can_i.py, which re-sanitises and additionally drops any figure whose label or value pattern-matches a sort code, an eight-digit account number or an IBAN, dropping the whole figure rather than redacting it, with tests proving a legitimate neighbouring figure survives. Cost is bounded by construction at roughly 200 to 300 tokens worst case and 60 to 90 on a realistic screen, and it cannot grow with a list because there is no list field. BALANCE HIDING IS RESPECTED CORRECTLY: when balances are hidden the figures are OMITTED entirely rather than masked and sent, while the verdict word is still published, which matches the screen, since the status chip is never blurred. Flow figures on Spend and Upcoming are not gated by hide-balances on screen either, consistent with DESIGN.md separating flows from positions, so sending them agrees with the screen rather than contradicting it. SafeToSpendCard keeps all five render paths including error, degraded and unsupported, never returns null, and its hero arithmetic is unchanged. No new tool, no write access, and the server-decided verdict rule was extended rather than weakened. Raised separately: its new test is genuine, proved by mutation, but is wired into no gate.
- [x] **B40. canPurchaseInApp resolves to web during server-side rendering because there is no Capacitor bridge in the Node build (noted by the B31 review, 2026-09-14, not fixed there): it is safe today only because every purchase surface happens to wait on an async fetch before rendering the control, which is an accident of the current code rather than a guarantee; make the native check resolve after mount, or carry an explicit unknown state that no purchase surface treats as web, so a future surface that renders synchronously cannot show a Stripe purchase path inside the native app** [owner: claude] (done 2026-09-18, 4f1dd057dbdf91cb41f6c30c1dd9f23911a4e805)
- [x] **B41. Tax is the one screen Penny can reach but cannot see, because no page ever publishes its view context** [owner: claude] (done 2026-09-18, ee596d5d21849d453fe3e0b086aeb6576761d9fc)
  - note (2026-09-15, claude): Found by the G86 review 2026-09-16, a pre-existing gap rather than anything G86 caused, and explicitly NOT a regression: setPennyScreenView has never been called for Tax, before or after that change. B39 added per-screen VIEW context so Penny knows not just which screen you are on but what it is showing, and wired it into Spend, Planning and the Safe-to-Spend card. Tax was not included. So a question asked from /tax now opens Penny with the right screen key and the right four chips, since G86 added the case "/tax" line to screenForPathname, but with no description of what the page is actually showing. The practical effect is that asking about the figure in front of you on Tax gets answered from raw tools rather than from the view, which is precisely the problem B39 was opened to fix. Tax is a good candidate for it: the page states a verdict, the 60 percent trap or a fully tapered allowance, and carries a small number of pre-formatted figures, which is exactly the bounded shape the VIEW contract expects, at most six figures as strings. Follow B39 pattern rather than inventing a second one, and respect the same rule it established, that when balances are hidden the figures are omitted rather than masked. Worth doing after the Canvas Before Cards programme settles, since the Tax page is being rebuilt underneath it.

- [x] **C16. The ios-capacitor-prod TestFlight build uploads with a build number BELOW the UAT workflow's, so TestFlight never offers it as the current build. Kevin ran the first production TestFlight build 2026-09-19 and App Store Connect shows it as 1.0 (1), expiring in 90 days, which makes it the newest upload, while the TestFlight app still offers 1.0 (36) under Currently Testing because 36 is the highest build number. Cause: codemagic.yaml's shared step_build_number anchor (line 377) stamps CFBundleVersion with Codemagic's $BUILD_NUMBER, which is per-workflow, so the separate ios-capacitor-prod workflow (line 437) started its own counter at 1 while ios-capacitor had already reached 36. Both workflows publish to the SAME App Store Connect app (bundle co.uk.auriqltd.sorted, shared app_store_connect integration and submit_to_testflight via the shared_app_store_connect anchor), so they share one build-number namespace and a per-workflow counter cannot stay monotonic across them. The fix is already named in that step's own trailing comment at line 399: read the last used number from App Store Connect with app-store-connect get-latest-testflight-build-number <app_apple_id> and increment it, instead of trusting $BUILD_NUMBER; apply it to the shared anchor so both workflows draw from the same source. Until fixed, the production build is installable only by picking build 1.0 (1) explicitly from the build list rather than the Currently Testing card. Note the same static CFBundleVersion of 1 is why the step exists at all, per its own comment, and the Android APK likewise reports versionCode=1, harmless today because nothing uploads Android to a store yet.** [owner: claude] [priority: p2] (done 2026-09-19, 457a508d118a6e218086bd29ac17db3bd8932559)

Privacy Policy link does nothing in the native app (Kevin 2026-09-22): Settings opens it with router.push('/privacy') at app/settings/SettingsPage.tsx line 1762 and Terms is wired the same way one button above, so both legal pages are very likely broken in the shells even though only Privacy has been reported; the page itself is fine, app/privacy/page.tsx is force-static and the current mobile bundle at capacitor-spike/www contains privacy.html at 82KB with real content from the same 2026-09-21 build as index.html, so this is a navigation fault in the shell, not a missing page; leading hypothesis, frontend/next.config.ts sets no trailingSlash so the export writes privacy.html rather than privacy/index.html, and Capacitor serves the local www directory by exact path with no .html fallback, so a route the Next client router cannot satisfy from its in-memory tree falls to a request for /privacy that the local server cannot resolve, which is the standard Capacitor-plus-Next-export failure and is fixed by enabling trailingSlash for the MOBILE_EXPORT build only, so the web deployment on Vercel is untouched; reproduce first on both an iOS and an Android build against the current bundle, confirm whether Terms fails identically and whether the footer links in components/AppOnlyPage.tsx do too, then fix the class by making every internal route resolvable in the shell rather than special-casing the two legal pages, and add a check to the mobile build script that fails when a linked internal route has no resolvable file in the export; this is p1 because both stores require the privacy policy to be reachable from inside the app and a reviewer who taps a dead link will reject the submission** [owner: claude] [priority: p1] [state: blocked: Parked by Kevin 2026-09-22 pending one device check. Everything establishable from source has been established and is in the note above. The single remaining unknown is whether tapping Privacy Poli...]
  - note (2026-09-23, claude): Investigation 2026-09-22, read-only, no fix applied. Three things, and the item's own proposed fix would not have worked. One, the trailingSlash claim is structurally right but insufficient. Next writes privacy.html rather than privacy/index.html because trailingSlash is unset, confirmed against Next's own export worker where subFolders follows trailingSlash. Two, the item's Capacitor mechanism is wrong and this changes what a fix must do. Capacitor's bundled WebViewLocalServer defaults html5mode to true and capacitor.config.json does not override it, so handleLocalRequest serves the ROOT index.html for any extensionless path without checking whether a real file exists there. The request therefore resolves to the wrong document rather than failing, which matches the link appearing to do nothing, and it means enabling trailingSlash alone would still not make /privacy resolve, because Capacitor never looks for the directory. A real fix needs either html5mode false, which changes error behaviour app-wide from silently misrouting to honestly 404ing, or a different navigation approach for these routes. Three, a separate real defect the item does not mention: components/AuthProvider.tsx gates exempt routes with a raw pathname equality check against /privacy and /terms and never uses normaliseNavPath from lib/navExemptRoutes.ts, the helper that exists precisely because Capacitor can hand the app /privacy.html. So even when the correct file loads, a non-canonical pathname falls th...
  - note (2026-09-23, claude): Continuation, the preceding note hit the 1500 character NOTE_CAP and was silently truncated mid-sentence. The cut sentence ends: a non-canonical pathname falls through to the signed-in gate and renders LoginScreen instead of the legal page. Three further findings were lost from the board entirely and are restored here. One, AppOnlyPage's Terms and Privacy footer links are moot, that component is never rendered in the mobile export because build-mobile.sh forces NEXT_PUBLIC_WEB_PRODUCT on, so nobody should fix them believing it affects mobile. Two, the OAuth callback routes are NOT excluded from the export as this item's own text claims, build-mobile.sh patches each one with force-static so they are included as zero byte extensionless files. Three, app/month/MonthPage.tsx around lines 255 to 259 uses a plain anchor rather than Link or router.push, so it hard-navigates and would hit the same Capacitor html5mode trap as the legal pages. The blocking prerequisite found during this investigation, the mobile export build failing outright on main, is fixed separately as C18.

Separately confirmed while doing this, and already on the board as H76: frontend/scripts/build-mobile.sh still cannot run inside a worktree, because its rsync excludes node_modules by directory pattern while in a worktree node_modules is a symlink, so it copies the symlink and the later ln -s fails with File exists. The build above therefore had to be run by mirroring the script's core step into a scratch directory rather than by invoking the script, which is a workaround and not a fix; H76 remains the item for that.
- [x] **C19. iOS push never arrives: APNs rejects every send with DeviceTokenNotForTopic and push.py prunes only BadDeviceToken/Unregistered, so the stale token retries forever** [owner: claude] [priority: p2] (done 2026-09-23, f9d638871d5767816de4cf6a5550f6efa6800dc2)
  - note (2026-09-23, claude): Evidence gathered 2026-09-23 before starting. Symptom: every native iOS push to kevin.maingi12@gmail.com fails, logged as 'APNs 400 ... DeviceTokenNotForTopic', repeating on each 4-hourly sync-worker run (journalctl -u wealth-worker, 04:00, 08:00, 12:00 on 2026-09-23). Two separate faults. (1) Dead-token pruning is incomplete: backend/app/core/push.py:90-99 prunes on 410 Unregistered and on 400 with reason in ('BadDeviceToken','Unregistered'), but every other 400 reason, DeviceTokenNotForTopic included, falls to the bare logging.warning at line 99 with no prune, so an undeliverable token is retried forever. (2) The stored token itself is wrong for the topic: apns_tokens holds one ios row for Kevin created 2026-08-22 15:46:59, 64 hex chars, and the send sets apns-topic to APNS_BUNDLE_ID (push.py:67), which UAT does not set in backend/.env so it falls back to the config default co.uk.auriqltd.sorted. That id matches capacitor.config appId and codemagic.yaml's bundle_identifier, so the topic is right and the TOKEN is the mismatch, most likely registered by an older build. Note codemagic.yaml:277,327 still carry a com.getcapacitor.App fallback in the entitlements patch, which is a plausible source of a token minted under the default Capacitor bundle id. Scope: fix the pruning so any permanent APNs rejection removes the token, and get a fresh token registered from a current build so Kevin actually receives notifications. Do not delete Kevin's row by hand without confirming the...
  - note (2026-09-23, claude): Tail of the previous note, which hit the 1500-char cap: ...do not delete Kevin's apns_tokens row by hand without first confirming the app re-registers a token on launch, or he silently gets nothing at all.
  - note (2026-09-23, claude): Fault-2 investigation (read-only, no DB writes). Registration: frontend/lib/capacitorPush.ts initCapacitorPush() calls PushNotifications.register() and POSTs the token via api.registerApnsToken on every 'registration' event; frontend/components/NativePushResync.tsx (mounted globally in frontend/app/layout.tsx:106) calls resyncCapacitorPush() -> initCapacitorPush() on EVERY app launch whenever OS permission is already granted, not only on first grant. So pruning the stale row is self-healing IF the installed app build's bundle id matches the currently configured APNs topic (co.uk.auriqltd.sorted): next launch re-registers and POSTs a fresh token, inserting a new apns_tokens row. Root cause of the mismatch, found via git log: capacitor.config.json's appId (and codemagic.yaml's bundle_identifier) was co.uk.auriqltd.wealth from 2026-08-06 until commit 67f9b3a8 (2026-09-05 18:25) renamed it to co.uk.auriqltd.sorted. Kevin's stale apns_tokens row was created 2026-08-22 15:46:59 (commits 9949bd8f/35633092, same day push was first enabled) - i.e. minted under the OLD topic co.uk.auriqltd.wealth, two weeks before the rename. Current sends use apns-topic=co.uk.auriqltd.sorted (the post-rename default), hence DeviceTokenNotForTopic. On codemagic.yaml:277,327's com.getcapacitor.App fallback: ios/ is never committed, npx cap add ios (codemagic.yaml:185) regenerates it fresh each build from capacitor.config.json's appId, which has never literally been com.getcapacitor.App in this repo's...

- [ ] **D10. Identity linking only goes one way and re-pointing strands an account's data (Kevin 2026-09-19): Settings can link an Apple sub to the signed-in account but there is no link-Google, so an Apple-first user has no way to attach their real email later, and when an explicit link re-points an automatic apple:{sub} away from the account that first created it, routers/auth.py's erase_orphaned_relay_account deliberately refuses if that account holds real data, which is correct as a safety rule but leaves a user who onboarded under Apple, connected banks and then linked from a fresh Google account signed into the empty one with everything left behind; add link-Google as the mirror of link-Apple keyed on Google's stable sub, and replace strand-by-default with a decision at link time, if the account being claimed away from has real data then either refuse with copy that names which account holds the data and tells the user to sign in there and link from that side, or offer a merge that moves accounts, transactions, preferences, subscriptions, allocations, commitments, card terms, linked identities and the connector's OAuth clients onto the surviving account under a single transaction with a backup, Kevin to choose between refuse and merge before build since merge touches every collection keyed on user_id; also add the pre-link prompt at Apple sign-up, already using Sorted with Google, sign in with that and add Apple from Settings, so the two-account situation is avoided before it exists** [owner: claude] [priority: p2]

- [x] **F18. Harden MCP tool descriptions so an external harness inherits Penny's quote-verbatim contract** [owner: claude] [priority: p2] (done 2026-09-14, 1c40ab09ff223340bb022b7823eae27d9363906f)
  - note (2026-09-14, claude): Found in the same 2026-09-14 harness audit. app/routers/mcp.py hands the same TOOL_SCHEMAS to an external harness such as Claude.ai, which has none of penny_agent.py's _SYSTEM_PROMPT. The rule that pre-formatted GBP must be quoted verbatim and that the model never does arithmetic lives only in that prompt, so through the connector an external harness can sum two of our figures and present the result as ours. Masking (mcp_mask) and scopes are solid, the semantic contract is not. Fix is per-tool description hardening, stating inside each tool's own description what must be quoted and what must not be recombined.

Either wire the time filter to the month parameter the endpoint already supports, including month=all, or remove the control; and either give non-assistant client identifiers a human label or exclude them from the filter, deciding first what a 'session' row even represents to a user reading their own audit log** [owner: claude] [state: blocked: superseded by F20, which covers these two faults and two more]

  - note (2026-09-16, claude): Coordinator 2026-09-18: independently reviewed, SHIP, with each of the four faults checked specifically rather than generally. Fault 1, the All time chip, is now a real Time range control and CRUCIALLY it re-fetches from the server, passing an explicit month parameter that routers/mcp.py narrows by year_month, rather than filtering the page already loaded, which is exactly the silently-wrong shortcut the old comment warned against. Fault 3 adds seconds to the timestamp, which disambiguates the two pairs Kevin reported; grouping repeats with a count was considered and rejected in-comment for a good reason, that a pair can straddle a cursor page boundary and grouping would hide which request a Details tap describes. The tool labels are done properly: all nineteen keys in the new label map were cross-referenced against the backend TOOL_SCOPES dict and match key for key, with an unmapped future tool degrading gracefully. FAULT 4 IS THE ONE WORTH RECORDING, because the right thing happened: the item said it needed reproducing, a documented repro attempt was made at 390 by 844 with a cold load, a throttled slow load while the page grew, and in-app navigation, scrollY stayed 0 throughout, and NO CODE WAS ADDED. A speculative fix to an unconfirmed bug would have been worse than leaving it. FAULT 2 IS A STATED DECISION, NOT A FULL FIX, and that is the honest framing: the UI now excludes session from the count and relabels it You, in the app, but routers/mcp.py:338 still returns cli...

  - note (2026-09-06, claude): Context (2026-09-06, Kevin + Fable): short_reason_for in backend/app/services/net_position.py splits short into bills (cash after set-asides <= 0, red, hero = full gap) and cards (cash positive, card growth pushes net below zero, amber, hero forced to £0 in frontend/components/SafeToSpendCard.tsx). Result: a £10 cash gap plus £100 on cards reads as £110 red, while bills covered plus £1,000 on cards reads as £0 amber, so the worse position looks calmer. Kevin's decision: show the net figure for the shortfall in the cards case too. Still to discuss when picked up: caption wording, whether chip severity escalates on the net figure or a threshold, and keeping bills-at-risk as its own red signal. Design change: propose to Kevin before building.

Backend: card_growth_unpaid reserve in routers/analytics.py step 6c and short_reason_for in services/net_position.py; frontend clamp in components/SafeToSpendCard.tsx.
