- [ ] **G41. Establish whether a predicted bill matching a PENDING debit should leave Safe-to-Spend's bills_total before it settles (leading hypothesis from the G35 investigation, which ruled out both code suspects with evidence): pending_transactions.py excludes a predicted bill from upcoming_bills/bills_total once it matches a freshly-observed pending debit, and task_reconcile_truelayer resyncs every four hours, so a bill going pending raises the headline figure with no settled transaction and nothing the user can see moved; confirm whether the pending debit has already reduced the account's available balance at that point (in which case the exclusion is correct and the figure is right) or has not (in which case the money is counted as free while still owed, and the bill should stay reserved until it settles); use the B18 daily snapshots once they have accumulated to name the exact field and bill that moved** [owner: claude] [state: blocked: awaiting Kevin's decision: (b) bill should stay reserved until it settles]
  - note (2026-09-11, claude): (b): bill should stay reserved until settled. TrueLayer docs: available = current + overdraft - pending; current does NOT net pending debits, only available does. Sorted stores current as accounts_col.balance (truelayer_sync.py:313-314, finexer_sync.py:603); Safe-to-Spend pools ONLY balance, never available (analytics.py:1175, 1115 _split_balances, via _safe_to_spend_accounts:3818). _match_pending_observed (analytics.py:3227) excludes the matched bill from bills_total once pending, on pending_transactions.py:5-16's premise that current/available both already reflect it -- true only for available. spendable_now (current) stays unchanged while bills_total drops: safe_to_spend rises by the full bill with nothing moved. No live example today: Kevin's only pending row is a GBP0 Klarna hold, matches no bill by amount; B18's safe_to_spend_history_col has 0 docs for every user. Recommend: stop excluding the bill in analytics.py:3213-3230, or prefer accounts_col.available (nets pending) over balance.
  - note (2026-09-11, claude): session abandoned, branch feature-G41-pending-bill-reserve discarded
  - note (2026-09-11, claude): Coordinator review of the 2026-09-11 investigation: its conclusion (b) rests on TrueLayer's documented formula (available = current + overdraft - pending) plus the fact that Sorted's spendable pool sums accounts_col.balance, which stores the provider 'current' field (truelayer_sync.py:313, analytics.py:1109-1122, projection at :1175 does not even fetch 'available'). But it found NO live example in Kevin's data (his only pending row is a 0.00 Klarna hold) and the B18 snapshot collection is still empty, and it directly contradicts the pending_transactions.py docstring, which says the opposite behaviour was CONFIRMED LIVE on 2026-09-01 via TrueLayer's pending endpoint, the phantom-shortfall bug the exclusion was written to fix. Banks differ: several UK banks move the ledger at authorisation so 'current' already nets pending. Do not act on documentation alone.
  - note (2026-09-11, claude): Decisive test before any change: for one real pending bill per provider on Kevin's accounts, capture accounts_col.balance AND the stored 'available' immediately before and after the pending row appears, and the settled drop afterwards; if balance is unchanged at pending time the exclusion double-counts and should be removed (or the pool should prefer 'available'), if balance already dropped the current behaviour is right and the 2026-09-01 fix stands. The answer may differ per bank, in which case the rule must be per-provider, not global. Safe-to-Spend is fail-closed by doctrine, so if this stays ambiguous the safer default is to keep the bill reserved until it settles.
  - note (2026-09-13, claude): Empirical check 2026-09-13 against Kevin's 23 real accounts, read-only: we store BOTH the provider's current and available balances, and six accounts differ. Five are credit cards where available is the remaining credit limit, nothing to do with pending. The sixth is a current account where available is TEN POUNDS HIGHER than current, an arranged buffer, not a pending debit. No account shows available BELOW current, which is the shape a pending debit would produce, and he has exactly one pending row, the zero-pound Klarna hold. So his live data cannot settle this today because there is no real pending debit in flight. What it does show is that available is not simply current minus pending in this data, so the two figures cannot be used interchangeably to infer pending. Likely resolution of the contradiction between the 2026-09-01 live observation and the 2026-09-11 documentation reasoning: a direct debit actually taken reduces CURRENT on the morning it is taken, which is what the original observation saw and what the exclusion was built for, whereas a card authorisation sits in pending and reduces only AVAILABLE. If that is right the rule should distinguish bill-shaped debits from card holds rather than treating all pending alike. Still needs the decisive capture: balance and available immediately before and after a real predicted bill goes pending.

- [x] **G58. Child benefit toggle hides save failures (found while fixing G52, 2026-09-12): handleChildBenefitToggle in app/settings/SettingsPage.tsx around line 665 calls api.updatePreferences({has_child_benefit: next}).catch(() => {}), so a failed save leaves the switch showing a value the server never stored, with nothing told to the user; it does NOT have the impure-updater fault because the state call and the API call are not coupled, so the fix is smaller than G45 and G52: revert on failure, surface it with the ink-plus-amber-dot pattern those two established, and guard the rawPrefs sync effect for this field the same way; check the income and pension handlers in the same file at the same time and say whether they share it** [owner: claude] (done 2026-09-12, 421b4d0a51a4ca440a8f87c79a714e7859a65cc9)
- [x] **G59. No separate work needed here; this item should be closed once G60 merges rather than picked up again.
- [x] **G60. Ten more preference writes still fail silently, the same class G45, G52 and G58 fixed one at a time (inventory taken by the G58 agent, 2026-09-12): components/PreferencesContext.tsx around lines 190, 196, 201, 206, 211 and 216 (hide balances, dark mode, pay period config, region, debt target months, debt tracking start), components/SpendTrends.tsx around 1030 and 1036 (spend widgets, home pinned widget) and components/Onboarding.tsx around 181 and 195 (pay period config, income value) all do an optimistic setState then api.updatePreferences(...).catch(() => {}) with no revert and nothing shown to the user, so a failed save leaves the app displaying a setting the server never stored; hide balances is the one that matters most because a user who turns balances off and is told it worked may still have them shown on another device; rather than three more one-at-a-time fixes, extract the shape those three items converged on (compute next outside any state updater, one write in flight per field, revert and surface on failure, reconcile from the server and fall back to the pre-change value when that also fails, participate in the preferences version scheme) into a single reusable helper and move all ten onto it, with tests** [owner: claude] [priority: p2] (done 2026-09-12, 94e86489c53af0b7a3edf2ba895170af40488c5f)
  - note (2026-09-12, claude): Supersedes doing G59 and any further one-off fixes in isolation: the helper is the point, not another individual patch. Related: G45 (5bf60ff), G52 (408ffbd), G58.
  - note (2026-09-12, claude): Two more findings from the G58 review to fold into the helper: the income and pension sync path has no in-flight guard, so a stale refetch landing mid-save can revert a just-typed figure, the same class this work keeps fixing elsewhere; and failure messages never auto-clear while success messages do, an asymmetry shared by financeMsg. The helper should settle both once for every field rather than leaving each site to decide.
- [x] **G61. GET /preferences returns two different shapes depending on whether a document exists (found while fixing G58, 2026-09-12): the no-document branch in routers/preferences.py around lines 51-71 omits income_bracket, income_value, pension_annual and has_child_benefit entirely, while the normal branch returns all four with defaults, so a brand new user gets a payload missing keys that client code now tests for with a presence check rather than truthiness (see the G58 sync effect); make both branches return the same key set with the same defaults, and add a test asserting the two shapes match** [owner: claude] (done 2026-09-12, 8653c566303f2924b53d346255f08acd56e7504b)
- [x] **G62. One field's failed save can stomp another field's in-flight write, because every reconcile goes through the whole-document loadPreferences (found by the G60 review, 2026-09-12): components/PreferencesContext.tsx loadPreferences is used both for mount hydration and as the reconcile callback for every field, and it re-applies ALL fields from whatever snapshot it receives, so if hide_net_worth is mid-PATCH while dark_mode's save fails, the refetch can carry the old hide_net_worth value, pass the version freshness gate because the in-flight PATCH has not bumped the version yet, and overwrite both the optimistic state and localStorage wd_hide_balances; the later success path only reports the version and never re-applies, so the wrong value sticks for the session and survives into localStorage until a full reload; the shape predates G60 but G60 widens it by adding five more failure-triggered refetch points and by bringing hide_net_worth into the reconcile machinery for the first time; fix by scoping each saver's reconcile to its own field, or by having loadPreferences skip any field whose saver reports isSaving, which createPreferenceSaver already exposes for exactly this purpose and G60 left unwired** [owner: claude] [priority: p2] (done 2026-09-12, afcb346a5f49aa295c065df71d99cb88b97f4eb8)
- [x] **G63. Settings reads the raw preferences payload for the balance mask instead of the context's typed value, so it can show a stale mask (reviewer observation on G62, 2026-09-12): app/settings/SettingsPage.tsx line 1468 uses hideAmounts={rawPrefs === null || Boolean(rawPrefs.hide_net_worth)} while every other consumer reads the context's hideNetWorth; rawPrefs is never updated on a SUCCESSFUL write either, since createPreferenceSaver's apply path sets state and localStorage but not rawPrefs, so this card can keep showing the old mask after the user has changed it, which predates G62 and was only ever self-healed by the whole-document reconcile G62 correctly stopped; switch that prop to the context's hideNetWorth, and audit the file for other direct rawPrefs reads that should be typed context values** [owner: claude] [priority: p2] (done 2026-09-12, bebaf7943256a63019e0cb7a5dba78410155d21e)
- [x] **G64. Cover plan sources card still describes the old three-class offline ranking, which G47 replaced (found by the G47 agent 2026-09-13, flagged rather than fixed because that file was out of its scope): components/CoverPlanSourcesCard.tsx line 40 says 'A manual-transfer account is checked last, after every connected account in both classes', which is now false, an offline current account is checked within the current class; its CoverOutcome helper around lines 97-128 also buckets every account.manual into one manual group regardless of class, so the summary contradicts the engine while the row-level grouping from G51 is already correct; update the copy and the helper to describe ranking by real class with the manual-transfer cost as a tie-break at equal headroom, and check no other safeguard sentence on that card makes a claim the engine no longer honours** [owner: claude] [priority: p2] (done 2026-09-13, 77a33a7e037af90fe9f4caec0d35099410766ab0)
- [x] **G65. Manual account class defaults in opposite directions in two places (reviewer major on G47, 2026-09-13, unreachable today): services/companion.py around lines 1545 and 1555 maps an offline account's account_type with 'CURRENT' if it is exactly current else 'SAVINGS', while routers/accounts.py's _manual_to_account around lines 92 and 102 maps 'SAVINGS' if it is exactly savings else 'TRANSACTION', so a value present but outside the three-value enum falls to savings in the engine and to current on the accounts API; every write path today is validated against ACCOUNT_TYPES in routers/manual_accounts.py, and routers/savings.py and routers/transactions.py both hardcode savings, so nothing can currently produce a value outside the enum, but the comment in companion.py claiming parity with _manual_to_account is false and this is exactly the engine-versus-screen drift G50 and G55 were opened to close; make one module import the classification from the other so the two cannot disagree by construction, rather than aligning the polarity by hand** [owner: claude] [priority: p2] (done 2026-09-13, cacf11588fee06da7233556b500b201dfdc793b4)
- [x] **G66. - [x] **G71. The cover-plan engine now decides whether a regular move card would be emitted in two separate places that agree only by hand** [owner: claude] [priority: p2] (done 2026-09-15, 751ddc77d4ef7410152a1770238dd59ecbd2e6c2)
  - note (2026-09-14, claude): Raised by the G70 backend review 2026-09-15, flagged rather than fixed because it was outside that item scope. backend/app/services/companion.py section 5d, around lines 2926 to 2960, runs a shadow simulation of whether the regular move card would be emitted, so that it can decide whether to suppress the separate unfunded_move card. The real emission gate lives in section 6, around lines 3204 to 3300. The reviewer traced both line by line and they agree exactly today, same _id formats, same dismissed, existing and cap checks, same iteration order over shortfalls, so nothing is wrong right now. The risk is entirely future: any edit to the real gate that is not mirrored in the shadow copy desyncs them silently, and the failure mode is the one G70 exists to fix, a user told to move money they do not need to move. Extract one shared predicate that both call rather than keeping two copies in step by hand. Note this is the same class as G55 and G65, where eligibility was decided independently on two sides and agreed only by coincidence; the fix there was to make one side authoritative by construction, and the same applies here.
  - note (2026-09-15, claude): Coordinator 2026-09-16: independently reviewed, SHIP. There is now genuinely ONE predicate, _gate_regular_move_cards, called once before section 5d, with both former duplicate sites only READING its result; a grep for the old duplicated identifiers finds nothing. Sizing and source selection are untouched, confirmed by grepping the diff for amount_needed, _ceil5, credited_incomes and _src_order and finding zero hits, so the engine cannot recommend a different amount or a different source than before. None of the G47, G50, G55, G65, G66 or G70 doctrine is touched. BETTER THAN THE ITEM CLAIMED: the two probes could already disagree TODAY without any future edit, because they were two independent awaited round-trips to Mongo and a concurrent write could make the second answer differently from the first. The one genuinely new test reproduces exactly that with a collection whose second lookup flips its answer, and it fails red against main with the overdue movement vanishing from both cards. So this closes a live race rather than only a maintenance hazard, and the desync class is now structurally impossible rather than merely tested for. One low-severity consequence raised separately: the shared call sits outside the try and except that used to wrap section 5d, so a transient error there now fails the whole brief instead of degrading one card.

- [x] **G73. Two small drift risks left by the G57 chart-defaults fix: the preview hard-codes the pinned widget, and a dead SpendHeader twin still carries the bug its sibling just fixed** [owner: claude] (done 2026-09-18, f2268152c1d9524025904c40b5cab4b824b2cb4e)

If the backend default ever changes, both previews go silently stale. Lower stakes than the original defect, since it only mislabels which chart reads as pinned in a mock rather than making a false numeric claim, but it is the same class. Second: frontend/components/SpendHeader.tsx:499, the default-exported SpendHeader, still computes hasMoved as moved_total !== undefined, the very bug G57 fixed in its sibling at :398. Since spend_verdict.py always returns moved_total as a number, zero rather than undefined, that condition is effectively always true, so the component can offer a jump to a Money you moved section that does not render. It is dead in production, only frontend/app/design/spend-period-round/Baseline.tsx imports it as a historical before preview, so there is no live user impact; the reason to fix it is that a known-wrong pattern sitting in the same file as its own correction is exactly what gets copied forward. Decide whether that Baseline preview still earns its place at all, and if it does not, delete the dead export with it.

- [x] **G77. Penny's cover-plan exclusion write still discards a concurrent Settings change, and the test meant to prove otherwise asserts the loss is correct** [owner: claude] [priority: p2] (done 2026-09-18, 2944dc09ab9aede308cf181fa94ea3a6083d1725)
  - note (2026-09-14, claude): Found by a post-merge review of G54 (52f81fc) on 2026-09-16. NOT EXPLOITABLE TODAY: Penny agent mode consent is off everywhere per G5, so nothing in production can hit this, which is why it is p2 and not p1. But it must be fixed before that consent is enabled anywhere. G54 shipped TWO different fixes for two write shapes and only one of them works. The Settings side is genuinely right: a single account toggle now sends cover_plan_exclude_add or cover_plan_exclude_remove and the server applies an atomic addToSet or pull with no read before write, so two toggles racing on one device or across two devices both survive, proven by two tests that fail red against the pre-fix code. The Penny side is not. set_cover_plan_exclusions still sends the whole array, now wrapped in a compare-and-set at backend/app/routers/preferences.py:99-160 that reads the version, writes conditioned on it, and retries up to eight times before a 409. On conflict it re-sends the IDENTICAL unmodified array it started with, never re-reading or merging. Traced concretely: Penny reads version 3 with [acc-old] and intends [acc-old, acc-new-penny]; a Settings toggle lands and adds acc-mid-race, version goes to 4; Penny conflicts, loops, re-reads and SEES acc-mid-race, then writes its stale target anyway and discards it. That is a resolved conflict rather than a prevented one, the concurrent writer is silently clobbered exactly as before, just deterministically instead of racily, and it is precisely the clobbering-data-it-never-saw failure G54 was raised to fix. WORSE, THE TEST BLESSES IT: test_cas_full_list_set_retries_past_a_concurrent_delta_and_still_lands at tests/test_cover_plan_exclusions_concurrency.py:253 PASSES against the pre-fix code, because old code does not recognise the delta key the test injects and writes it as an unused literal, so the injected concurrent change never touches the array and the old unconditioned write lands on the asserted state by coincidence. Its own comment at lines 279-281 then calls the discarded write intentional documented semantics. So the lost update was rationalised rather than caught, and the test cannot fail either way. Same shape as A33. FIX: either make the CAS retry genuinely merge, re-deriving the target from the freshly read array rather than resending a stale literal, or re-scope Penny proposal to the same delta shape Settings now uses, which is simpler and makes the two writers agree by construction. FINDING A: invalidateMoneyShapeCache has exactly ONE caller in the whole frontend, AuthProvider's logout; nothing calls it from CategoriesContext, even though moneyShape.ts's own comment names category-kind edits as the trigger the server cache reacts to. Reclassify a category and /spend/shape shows the pre-edit split for up to 90 seconds. FINDING B, worse: invalidateVerdictCache has only two callers, logout and SpendPage's handleTxUpdated, and the fetch effect at SpendPage.tsx:380-391 depends only on [periodOffset, fetchVerdict], so it never re-runs when payPeriodConfig changes. Move payday mid-cycle and the cached verdict shows the OLD period's spend against the NEW period, a four-figure misrepresentation that persists across client-side navigation for the full TTL. - [x] **G84. The unified cover-plan gate now sits outside the error handling that used to isolate one of its two callers** [owner: claude] (done 2026-09-18, dbe32d621613984d03a7c455515e713a8f055693)
  - note (2026-09-15, claude): Raised by the G71 review 2026-09-16, flagged rather than fixed because G71 is a good change and this is a narrow consequence of it. Before G71, section 5d of backend/app/services/companion.py ran its own shadow probe inside a try and except, so a transient database error there degraded only the unfunded_move card while section 6 own independent probe still ran and could still render move cards. G71 correctly replaced both probes with one shared call, but that call now sits OUTSIDE that try and except, so a transient error in it is uncaught and fails the whole compute_today_items request: no move cards, no unfunded_move card, nothing. The author comment reasons about this and calls it no worse than section 6 existing failure mode, which is true as far as it goes but omits that section 5d failure used to be isolated and no longer is. Narrow, only reachable on a transient database error, and not a money-movement bug. Decide whether the shared gate should catch and degrade, returning an empty gate result so the brief still renders what it can, or whether failing the whole request loudly is actually preferable for a surface that tells someone to move money, since a partially rendered brief built on a failed lookup could be worse than none. Either is defensible; the current state is a side effect rather than a decision.

But SpendContainmentClient.tsx:282 reads the variant from ?variant= and accepts only b or c, falling back to a; ?state= is read separately at :284 as a VERDICT state from its own list, falling back to normal. So the index generates ?state=a, b or c, the client ignores it, and all three index entries render variant A. The same mistake is in the uat link recorded on G78. Fix by registering the three as variants rather than states, matching how the client actually reads them, or by changing the client to read ?state= if that is the intended contract, but one of the two must move so the index and the client agree. WORTH GENERALISING: this cannot be caught by checking status codes, because the switch happens client-side after hydration, so every combination returns 200 with byte-identical server-rendered HTML; it is only visible by reading the client own parameter parsing or by screenshotting each link and comparing. G57 had the same class of problem when its parameters changed from variant to charts and surface mid-round and the recorded link went stale. Consider whether the design-index check script, which already enforces that every preview directory is indexed, could also assert that each registered parameter name is one the client actually reads.

Fix by moving those three from the states array to the new variants array and giving states whatever verdict state the client actually accepts, which its own parsing shows defaults to normal. Three rounds have shipped with links that cannot reach their own variants: G78 registered its three as states while the client read variant, G89 read a position parameter while the index sent state, and G100 registered no variants or states at all so only variant A was reachable. G86 added the correct mechanism, a separate variants field on the index entry, and G87, G91 and the Tax round use it properly, so the capability is not missing, the older shape is simply being copied forward. Since the design index already has a check script enforcing that every preview directory is registered, the cheapest durable fix is to extend that check to assert that each registered parameter name is one the route client actually reads, which would catch all three of these at finish time rather than at review. That is a bigger change than the one-line migration this item was opened for, so do both: migrate spend-containment now, and consider the check separately.

Worth doing before Kevin picks from any of them, since a pick made without the nav may not survive contact with it.

- [x] **G103. Debt trajectory card leads with the full carried balance, which is demoralising and is the wrong measure (Kevin 2026-09-16): the card on Home renders the headline 'The cards aren't coming down at your current pace, £24,926 carried across 6 cards' and then repeats £24,926 as the hero figure, so the biggest number on the card is a stock the user cannot change this month; lead on the movement instead, the rate at which the balance is rising or falling, which is also what the product's own doctrine already treats as the needle, and keep the total as quiet supporting context rather than the headline; the movement figure already exists elsewhere on Home, the Safe-to-Spend card shows what went on cards this pay period, so reuse that series rather than deriving a second one, and make the card read as progress or drift over a stated window (this pay period, or the last three pay periods) with the direction carried in the words and not only in colour; red stays reserved for genuine risk per DESIGN.md, and a rising balance on 0% deals is not the same risk as one accruing interest, so the wording must distinguish them; copy lives in services/companion.py's trajectory item** [owner: claude] [priority: p2] (done 2026-09-22, 7acc558d270aa311609670fba9daec4cd45635bf)
  - note (2026-09-19, claude): Kevin re-raised this 2026-09-19 from a screenshot of his live Home, independently restating the item's own conclusion: the debt-trajectory card shouts £24,896 at him while the 'went on cards this pay period' line on Safe to Spend conveys the same movement without the glaring number.   - note (2026-09-22, claude): Scoped 2026-09-22 before starting. Three findings from reading the code. (1) The movement series the brief says to reuse CANNOT express progress. analytics.py line 3962 computes card_growth_total = round(max(0.0, sum(net_change)), 2), clamped at zero deliberately because Safe-to-Spend's reserve must fail closed, so it can only ever show drift, never a balance coming down. A card asked to read as 'progress or drift over a stated window' therefore needs a net figure that can go negative, computed from the same card_rows and the same window so it is still one series and not a second derivation, without disturbing the clamped total the reserve depends on. (2) There are two candidate series and they mean different things: card_new_spend_total (analytics.py line 3973, purchases only, balance transfers excluded) is what the Home hero's 'went on cards' line actually shows, while card_growth_total is true balance growth including money deliberately moved onto a card. For 'are the cards coming down' the honest measure is the latter, since a transferred balance is still debt owed; the brief points at the hero line, so this needs stating rather than silently picking one. (3) The 0%-versus-interest distinction the brief requires IS buildable from existing data: on_promo, promo_kind, promo_end and apr_pct are already read from the card doc by the promo-cliff item immediately above the trajectory item at companion.py lines 4385 to 4415, so no new data is needed. Also noted: G134, phase 1...

- [x] **G108. Tapping a nav icon jumps to the bottom of the page because push-versus-back detection uses window.history.length (Kevin 2026-09-16, reproduced by reading components/ScrollReset.tsx): the effect computes isPop as length <= lastHistoryLength, but history.length never shrinks on a back navigation, and a forward navigation after a back truncates the forward entries and appends one, leaving the length unchanged, so the very next nav tap is misread as a back and restoreScroll puts the user at the position they last left on that route; the failure is permanent once the tab reaches the browser's history cap because the length then never grows again. Kevin's stated requirement: going back remembers where he was, going forward starts at the top. Replace the length heuristic with something that actually distinguishes the two, for example stamping a monotonic index into history.state on each push and comparing it on navigation, and keep the existing height-gated restore that lib/scrollRestore.ts documents at length, since that part solves a real problem with late-arriving content. This also explains F20 fault 4, which an agent could not reproduce because it only tested cold loads and a single forward click, never a back followed by a forward** [owner: claude] [priority: p1] (done 2026-09-16, 57715ec18229f643c84cf5ee8301010d7b4ead11)
  - note (2026-09-16, claude): One gap left deliberately: the new npm run check:scroll-nav-detect is registered in package.json but NOT wired into scripts/session.sh's finish gate, because that file is shared infrastructure outside this item's scope. So the guard exists but does not run automatically, which is the same shape as the check:legal-content gap that let F14's privacy leak reach review. Worth wiring alongside check:design-index, check:nav-coverage and check:bank-logos in the same pass that merges this.

The Next.js contract this fix rests on was verified against the installed next 16.3.4 rather than taken on trust: preserveCustomHistoryState is false at segment-cache/navigation.js:350 and :461 and true at :492, and app-router.js's HistoryUpdater at :38-68 is the ONLY call site of native pushState/replaceState for app-router navigations, so there is no push path that bypasses the stamp-stripping contract. Kevin's acceptance test was walked by hand and holds, including the exact sequence he hit, back to a route then tapping a nav icon, which now starts at the top. The new test was run standalone and the OLD length algorithm, ported side by side, genuinely fails its critical assertion while the new one passes all eleven, so it is a real regression test and not one shaped to suit the fix. THREE RESIDUALS, none touching the reported bug. First, npm run check:scroll-nav-detect is registered in package.json but is NOT wired into scripts/session.sh's finish gate, so the regression test does not run automatically; noted on H49. Second, a dormant hole: refresh-reducer.js:75 routes through completeSoftNavigation with preserveCustomHistoryState false regardless of push or replace, so router.refresh() or a Server Action revalidation would strip the stamp off the entry the user is currently sitting on, and a later back to it would be read as a fresh push. It is not reachable today, there are no router.refresh call...
- [x] **G109. A bill charged to a credit card is counted against cash twice, once when it hits the card and again when the card is repaid (Kevin 2026-09-16, seen on Upcoming: a £180 Anthropic subscription on the Amex drops projected cash from £603 to £423 even though no cash moves, while the separate Amex repayment from Barclays also reduces it): routers/analytics.py already has the right predicate, is_assessable_bill at line 1063 requires a bill NOT be on a credit card and its own docstring calls it the single source of truth for three simulations that must agree byte for byte, but the Safe-to-Spend window at line 3832 and the Upcoming projection do not use it, they subtract every bill in the window whatever account it sits on; apply the cash-led rule that Kevin settled in G16 to the bills walk as well, so a charge on a card does not reduce cash, a payment TO a card does, and a movement between two pooled accounts does not, which is three categories separated by the pool boundary rather than by whether something is called a transfer; the boundary already exists, _account_pool_kind returns nothing for any account whose type or subtype contains credit and _is_pooled_spendable_transfer only excludes a movement whose destination is itself spendable, so a card repayment is structurally not a pooled transfer and must stay in the forecast; the dangerous failure mode is excluding card-related movements wholesale, which would drop the repayment too and mean cash never falls for card spending at all, worse than today because it would overstate what the user can spend; edit the shared predicate only, never a call site, keep the existing card-growth reserve as the fail-closed fallback for a card that is growing with no forecast repayment, verify the repayment is reliably predicted before removing the charge, and note that the forecast will improve overnight without the user being better off so the copy must not read as a windfall** [owner: claude] [priority: p1] (done 2026-09-16, 10032f447be1e3290e4ad35049ca2399005f66ef)

  - note (2026-09-18, claude): The G128 preview round depends on this bug. Its variants detect a savings-pot habitual top-up with the heuristic target === 0 && move > 0, which is only true because of defect (1) above. When this item fixes target for savings destinations, Kevin's Barclays 'Personal GBP' row will fall through to the normal branch and render 'Needs £100, has £100, moving £100', which is incoherent. Whoever fixes this must add an explicit savings or top-up flag to each destination in the payday_plan payload and update frontend/app/design/g128-payday-reconcile/shared.tsx (and whatever production code the winning variant becomes) to key off that flag instead of the target === 0 heuristic. The dependency is recorded in a comment on isHabitualTopUp in that file.

  - note (2026-09-22, claude): CORRECTION 2026-09-22 to the earlier note on this item claiming it is the cache half of G139. That is wrong and should not be acted on. GET /today is served by a SERVER-side Mongo-backed response cache in backend/app/routers/companion.py, checked before compute_today_items is ever called, with a 6 hour TTL and version-bumped invalidation. G138's fix is exclusively a client-side module-cache invalidator in frontend/lib/accountMutations.ts and cannot reach or bump the server cache by construction, so it cannot prevent a stale /today payload. Investigation also found the server cache was not in fact the culprit, since routers/transactions.py's update_transaction already calls response_cache.ainvalidate(uid) on every correction. What Kevin most likely saw was G139's bad data, correctly computed and correctly served, not a caching fault. One genuinely open client-side gap was found and is filed separately, see the new item referenced in G139's notes: frontend/components/TransactionSheet.tsx's correction flow calls no client cache invalidator at all, on main or on the G138 branch, while lib/homeCache.ts holds a companionItems warm-paint snapshot that a correction never clears.

- [ ] **G147. G139's merchant-recency fix degrades silently and permanently once a correction passes one year old, and nothing would tell us if the bug came back, raised by the independent review of G139 on 2026-09-22: G139 made a user's latest merchant decision win by ordering corrections on their teaching event timestamp from teaching_events_col, falling back to the transaction's own purchase date when no event exists, but teaching_events_col carries a 365 day TTL index (expireAfterSeconds 31536000, see the index setup in backend/app/main.py) while custom_category itself never expires, so this is NOT a one time gap for legacy rows as G139's own code comments frame it, it is a rolling degradation in which every correction eventually ages out for every user, meaning any two corrections to the same merchant that are both more than a year old fall back to purchase date ordering and can reinstate exactly the stale answer G139 fixed, and the users most exposed are the longest lived ones whose oldest corrections are the most likely to be wrong; the fix is observability first rather than changing the TTL, namely distinguish in logs or a counter between the two reasons the fallback fires, an event that genuinely expired versus a transaction that was never corrected, so the gap is measurable before it produces a second Playtomic style report, and only then decide between lengthening or removing the TTL on correction events specifically, persisting a durable per transaction decision timestamp such as custom_category_at written whenever custom_category is set, or accepting the degradation with the reasoning recorded; a second, much smaller finding from the same review belongs on this item too, backend/app/routers/transactions.py's resolve-movement spending branch at roughly lines 745 to 772 fires _log_teaching_event for movement_spending even on its true no-op path where category_changed stays False and custom_category is never written, which is harmless today because a row with custom_category None never enters Pass 4's override set, but it weakens the invariant that every teaching event corresponds to a real decision and could inflate a row's apparent decision recency if that row's custom_category is later set by another path.** [owner: claude]

- [x] **H46. integrate.py writes raw pytest output into an item's block note, which is the H38 corruption returning by the same route** [owner: claude] [priority: p2] (done 2026-09-15, 0c8cdccf8bfcfd783a53db10939a0be732dab5e7)
  - note (2026-09-13, claude): Found 2026-09-15 while reading G57. When integrate.py blocks an item on a failing backend suite it writes the captured pytest stdout straight into the block reason, so the board grows notes that read "backend test suite failed: / ........ [ 3%] / ........ [ 6%]" for hundreds of characters, carrying no information at all: the dots are progress output, and the actual failing test name and assertion are buried or truncated away. G57 and B29 both carry one right now, and H38 previously had to repair 24 lines of exactly this. The board is what Kevin reads at /ops/go-live, so these notes are pure noise on the page they are meant to inform. Fix at the source in scripts/integrate.py rather than by cleaning up after it: capture the pytest output, extract only the short test summary lines, the FAILED test ids and their assertion text, and write at most a few lines into the note, keeping the full log on disk or in the journal if it is wanted. Then repair the existing corrupted notes. Worth checking whether the same unbounded-capture path is used for frontend build failures and lint failures, which would corrupt the board the same way.
  - note (2026-09-15, claude): Second half done 2026-09-16: repaired the two corrupted notes the review found still standing, B33 and G57 (originally item text said B29, that was wrong, both note-level and cross-referenced: B29 carries no such note, B33 and G57 do, each with the identical pre-H46 raw-pytest dump cut off mid test body at "pack = _pack(UID, remaining=250"). Chose APPEND a correction over rewrite-in-place, following the C13/C14 precedent rather than H38: H38's damage was structural, dangling state tags and stray lines that broke the parser, so repair_todo (the H38 lint/apply tool) rewrites those in place by design, and its own docstring says it deliberately never touches a well-formed note. Our two notes parse fine; they are just low-information. backlog.py also has no command to replace an existing note's text at all, only note (append), so rewriting in place would have meant adding a new capability purely to save space, cutting against the board's append-only audit-trail design for no real gain. Recovered the diagnosis the corrupted notes cut off before reaching: both failures are the same test, test_get_subscription_serves_mcp_block_and_packs in backend/tests/test_mcp_call_packs.py, failing on assert result["billing_live"] is False (actual True) because it read the ambient BILLING_ENABLED flag B25's Stripe sandbox bring-up had just flipped true in backend/.env, rather than a pinned value; this is the exact bug B34 already diagnosed and fixed (commit 9a0f444422b6222e04a1d0b81dfb1d12534f6a53, test now pins via patch_billing_enabled). Verified by reading the current test file, not guesswork. Added one clarifying follow-up note on B33 since its first correction said "the note directly above" when a same-dated unrelated note actually sat between them; G57's correction was worded to quote the corrupted note's exact opening text instead, avoiding the ambiguity from the start. Corrupted-note grep count (20+ dot run) unchanged at 2 before and after by design, both left intact as the historical record; no other item in TODO.md carries the shape. backend/.venv/bin/python -m pytest tests/test_backlog.py tests/test_integrate_script.py -q: 190 passed. No code changed, board-only repair via scripts/backlog.py note from the shared tree; worktree is clean and already in sync with origin.

But npm run check:penny-screen-views is wired into neither scripts/session.sh finish nor scripts/integrate.py, both of which only run check:design-index and check:legal-content. Decide whether these belong in session.sh finish, in integrate.py before the merge, or in both, and note the cost: integrate already runs the whole backend suite before merging ANY item, so adding slow checks there blocks the entire board when one fails, which argues for finish rather than integrate.   - note (2026-09-15, claude): Raised by the G78 review 2026-09-16, a gap rather than a defect, and not a reason to have held that merge. That scoping was fine when almost everything lived on a card. G78 has now made the page canvas the default surface for orientation and explanation, so a growing share of the app figures will sit on canvas, where no rule states their weight at all. This is not hypothetical: the G57 review already observed that the new Spend journey money figures read LIGHTER than the headline sentence above them, in a container the doctrine explicitly calls plain canvas rather than a card, which sidesteps the rule as written. That observation was recorded and left, and G78 has now generalised the pattern it came from. Decide whether the Numbers Lead Rule should be rescoped from "on any card" to any surface presenting a financial figure, or whether canvas figures deserve a different and deliberately quieter treatment because they are narrative rather than instrument. Either is defensible and it is a Kevin-facing question about what the app sounds like, not a mechanical fix; what is not defensible is the current state, where the rule simply stops applying at the moment the container disappears. Note the PRODUCT.md north star that verdicts lead, which argues the figure should still carry weight wherever it sits.
- [ ] **H51. integrate.py rebuilds the frontend in place in the shared tree, so an interrupted or overlapping build leaves the live UAT site serving a broken build** [owner: claude] [priority: p1]
  - note (2026-09-16, claude): Raised 2026-09-17 after a real UAT outage Kevin reported as a blank white page. SEQUENCE OF EVENTS. At 01:33 a build wrote into /root/ai-wealth-dashboard/frontend/.next and did not finish: BUILD_ID was absent and .next/standalone/server.js was absent, while build, server, static and the manifests were present, which is the shape of a build interrupted partway. The already-running next start process kept serving from its in-memory manifest, so the site stayed up but degraded: the cached pre-rendered HTML for / referenced content-hashed chunks that no longer existed on disk, requests for them returned 500 with content type text/plain, the browser refused to execute a script with that MIME type, React never hydrated, and the page rendered genuinely blank with an empty body and no thrown exception. /design rendered but completely unstyled because its CSS chunk 404d despite existing on disk. Restarting the service then killed the only process that could serve anything, and next start refused to boot at all without BUILD_ID, so the site went from degraded to fully down and crash-looped 25 times before it was stopped. Restored by rebuilding in the shared tree and starting the service, verified in a real browser: / now renders the styled sign-in card and /design renders with styling. THE ROOT PROBLEM IS STRUCTURAL, not the interrupted build itself. scripts/integrate.py rebuilds the frontend IN PLACE in the shared tree, which is the same directory the live wealth-frontend service r...
- [ ] **H52. The frontend is built as standalone output but served with next start, which Next warns against on every boot** [owner: claude]
  - note (2026-09-16, claude): Noticed 2026-09-17 while diagnosing the blank-page outage; NOT the cause of it, and deliberately not changed during the incident. frontend/next.config.ts:221 sets output to standalone for any self-hosted build, meaning anything that is not a Vercel build and not the mobile export. The systemd unit at /etc/systemd/system/wealth-frontend.service runs /usr/bin/node node_modules/.bin/next start -p 3030. Next logs a warning on every single boot: next start does not work with output standalone configuration, use node .next/standalone/server.js instead. It has evidently worked anyway for weeks, so this is not urgent, but two things make it worth settling. First, running a configuration the framework explicitly warns against means the failure modes are undocumented, and during this outage a missing chunk returned 500 with content type text/plain rather than a clean 404, which is what made the page blank rather than merely broken; whether that ungraceful behaviour is related is unproven but plausible. Second, it means the build produces .next/standalone/server.js that nothing ever runs, so the intent is ambiguous to the next person reading it. Decide one way or the other: either serve with node .next/standalone/server.js as Next intends, updating the unit and checking the static and public asset copying that standalone requires, or stop building standalone for this host so the config matches how it is actually served. Do not do this during an incident.

Instructions alone are clearly not holding, so make it structural: give agents a documented way to start and stop a scratch server that cannot match anything else, for example a helper that records its own PID to a file in the worktree and kills only that PID, and have the worktree session tooling clean up any leftover PID on finish; also add the rule to the finish gate or a lint so a script containing a broad pkill cannot be committed** [owner: claude]

- [x] **H55. The /ops/go-live board is unusable on a phone: every scroll that starts on a card either does nothing or turns into a drag, because BoardView.tsx puts touch-none (touch-action: none) on every card while the dnd-kit TouchSensor activates after only 200ms, so the browser can never scroll from a card and an ordinary scroll gesture becomes an accidental state change (Kevin, 2026-09-16); make vertical scrolling always win, make a drag deliberate, and add an explicit Move to state picker to ItemDetailSheet covering every state so an accidental move can be set back by hand, which is the recovery Kevin asked for** [owner: claude] [priority: p2] (done 2026-09-16, 0d3c9de56c8b2abc43d474cf22e7bb8c09b42f97)
  - note (2026-09-16, claude): Rejected 2026-09-16 on review of the H55 branch feature-H55-the-ops-go-live (commit a60ba7f). The touch-action work in BoardView.tsx is correct and is not in question: touch-manipulation plus a 400ms/5px activation constraint is the right pairing for a delay-constrained dnd-kit TouchSensor, and the finding that touch-none was also silently blocking the mobile lane's own horizontal scroll is a good catch. The defect is in the Move to picker. The implementing agent found and documented that the backend's set_state and set_rejected never clear the done flag, and that to_dict always reports state done while that flag is true, so on a done item the In progress, Blocked and Rejected chips fire an action that has no visible effect. It mirrored the existing drag mapping rather than fixing it. That is the right call for drag, which is a shortcut, but not for this picker, which is the whole reason H55 exists: Kevin asked for a way to set a state back by hand after an accidental move, and marking something done by accident is the easiest slip to make on a phone. A control that appears to work and does nothing is worse than no control. Fix: when the item is currently done and the target state is anything other than done, fire reopen first and then the target action, in sequence, the same two-step shape fireOptimistic already uses in BoardView.tsx for owner-then-column drops. Also under review in the same pass: the picker drops the sheet's standalone Start and Move to To do buttons, wh...

Also folded into the same correction round: instrument set_done when it clears the done flag, because the refusal message and the docs both steer operators to reopen as the correct way to un-tick, and the Done to To do drag maps to reopen too, so the most likely accidental path out of Done is currently the one with no audit trail; map the raw state key in the note copy to the display label the board uses everywhere else, so it reads To do rather than todo; add the force flag to the CLI usage block in docs/ops/BACKLOG.md, which still shows the three commands without it; and delete the now-redundant _cli_show test helper in favour of _run_cli.

  - note (2026-09-17, claude): Coordinator 2026-09-17: NRestarts IS NOT A DURABLE RECORD. It read 5 after the five SIGKILLs, then read 0 immediately after the H56 integrate pass, because systemd resets the counter on an explicit restart and every integrate pass restarts wealth-frontend. So a later reading of 0 does NOT mean the kills never happened, and a recurrence could be wiped before anyone sees it. Whatever fixes this item should not rely on NRestarts; journalctl -u wealth-frontend is the durable evidence, and the kills are recorded there as code=killed status=9/KILL at 07:53:07, 07:54:56, 07:55:33, 07:57:06 and 07:59:27 on 2026-09-17.
- [ ] **H64. Notes are silently truncated at the 1500 character NOTE_CAP with no warning to the caller, which is the sibling of H54's 200 character cap on reject reasons and needs its own fix because H54's will not touch this path: it hit four times in a single session on 2026-09-17, and the fourth was the worst shape it can take, cutting the operational half of a note (the worktree path and the exact commands to resume an item) while preserving the prose rationale, so the board carried the reasoning and lost the instructions, which is the least recoverable failure mode. The damage is silent in every case: _collapse_note_text truncates and appends an ellipsis, the write succeeds, and nothing tells the caller their content was cut, so an agent believes it recorded something it did not. Options worth weighing rather than assuming: raise or remove the cap, since the original justification was preventing multi-line command output from corrupting the item line and that is already handled separately by newline collapsing; or split an over-long note into a numbered sequence of notes automatically so nothing is lost; or at minimum return the truncation to the caller so it can react, which is the cheapest and would have turned all four of this session's losses into a retry. Whichever is chosen, the same treatment should be applied to every other silently capped field on the board, so audit them together rather than one at a time** [owner: claude]
  - note (2026-09-23, claude): Live instance 2026-09-23: C17's investigation note was silently truncated at exactly 1500 characters, cutting mid-sentence and dropping three distinct findings off the board with no warning at the call site and no indication in the command's own success output. The caller reported success, and a second agent inspecting the result afterwards reported the note as untruncated, so the loss was only caught by measuring the stored string directly. That is the failure mode this item describes and it cost a real recovery: the findings had to be re-added as a second note. Worth noting alongside H54's 200 character reject-reason cap, since both silently destroy a reviewer's or investigator's reasoning; the minimum fix is for the writer to warn or fail rather than quietly cut.
- [ ] **H65. Design previews of breakpoint-specific components render at the reviewer's viewport width rather than the width they are meant to represent, so a mobile-only component opened on a laptop renders in a state that can never occur in production: /design/ops-board-mobile shows this, where the ribbon strip pins at top-0 and disappears entirely underneath the filter bar at 1280px because the preview's variant switch is not breakpoint-gated while production gates the component behind an isDesktop check, so the picked variant's defining element is invisible to anyone reviewing on a desktop. H56 shipped a visible note telling the reader to view at a phone width, which is a label rather than a fix. The real solution is to render a mobile-only preview inside a width-constrained iframe so its media queries resolve against the frame rather than the window, which would make every such preview show the truth at any reviewer viewport; worth doing generically in the design harness rather than per preview, and worth checking which other previews under frontend/app/design/ have the same latent problem** [owner: claude]

- [ ] **H76. frontend/scripts/build-mobile.sh cannot run inside a worktree at all (found during H75, 2026-09-17): its rsync copies the node_modules symlink into the scratch directory and the subsequent ln -s then fails under set -e, so the script aborts. Building the mobile export from a worktree is now a routine operation, since every session works in one and the Board APK is built from the export, so this blocks the normal path and was worked around locally with a temporary uncommitted patch rather than shipped. ln -sfn appears to be the fix; verify that the resulting symlink points where the shared-tree case expects and that the scratch cleanup still removes it** [owner: claude] [priority: p2]
  - note (2026-09-17, claude): Worse than originally boarded, found during the H79 APK rebuild on 2026-09-17. The failure is not a clean abort: because frontend/node_modules is a symlink in a worktree, rsync copies the symlink into the scratch directory and the following unguarded ln -s then FOLLOWS it and plants a stray node_modules symlink INSIDE THE SHARED TREE'S REAL node_modules, pointing back at the worktree, rather than erroring under set -e as assumed. So a script that is supposed to fail harmlessly in a worktree instead writes into /root/ai-wealth-dashboard, which is the one tree every session is forbidden to touch and which the live wealth-frontend service runs from. It was caught only because the agent inspected the shared tree after its first build, and removed it after confirming it was a bare symlink and not a directory tree; nothing else was affected and the shared tree is clean. Raised to p2 for that reason. ln -sfn is the known fix and was applied locally and reverted rather than committed, but the fix should also verify the resulting symlink points where the shared-tree case expects, confirm the scratch cleanup removes it, and add a guard so the script refuses to write outside its own scratch directory at all rather than relying on ln's behaviour. Worth checking whether any other script in scripts/ or frontend/scripts/ has the same rsync-then-ln-into-a-symlinked-path shape.

- [ ] **H81. Board dispatcher: run the build, review and merge loop from board state changes instead of from a live conversation (Kevin, 2026-09-17). Kevin's trigger is moving a ticket to in-progress himself, which is unambiguous because in-progress with NO branch is exactly the shape approve leaves and the shape session.sh start already accepts, while in-progress WITH a branch means a session is genuinely live on it, so the dispatcher can tell a hand-off from running work without inventing a state. The loop: Kevin moves an item owned by claude to in-progress with no branch, the dispatcher starts a headless build agent in a worktree via the claude CLI at /root/.local/bin/claude, the agent finishes into review with a branch, the dispatcher launches a reviewer agent, a passing review merges to main so it lands on UAT, and a failing review rejects with a reason and re-dispatches the builder within the attempt cap. Kevin's constraints: auto-merge to UAT only, production stays manual via release.py; one agent run at a time; two attempts per item, after which it parks and notifies him through the existing FCM path. MANDATORY SAFETY RAILS, because this removes the human judgement that currently bounds spend: a kill-switch file checked before every dispatch, a dry-run mode that logs what it would do without spending anything, structured logging of every dispatch with its token cost, a per-item attempt counter persisted on the board rather than in memory, and the auto-merge step behind an enable flag that ships OFF so the loop can be watched end to end before it is allowed to merge anything. Note two live hazards this amplifies: H78, where an integrate pass twice overtook a rejection because the merge decision and the state read are not atomic, which an automated rejector will hit far more often than a human did; and the relay-chain failure seen repeatedly on 2026-09-17, where an agent told to do the work instead spawned another agent and ended its turn, producing nothing but forwarded messages at real cost, so the dispatcher must verify a run actually produced a commit rather than trusting that it finished. Claude Code hooks are NOT the mechanism, they fire on tool events inside a running session and nothing external can raise them; this is a systemd service and timer alongside the existing integrate.py --loop which is already documented in docs/ops/BACKLOG.md but never installed** [owner: claude] [priority: p2]

The failure mode is worse than the tax, because a careless renumber can point an allowlist entry at a genuinely unsafe raise and the test will pass. Key it on a marker comment at the raise site, or on the enclosing function name, so it survives ordinary edits** [owner: claude]

This actually happened 2026-09-18 on G127: a stale, never-cleaned worktree /root/worktrees/feature-G127-upcoming-round3 sat at the rejected commit af861cd2 alongside the live /root/worktrees/feature-G127-round3-fix, and the first finish call resolved to the stale one, pushed the branch that had already been REJECTED on review, and marked the item review against it. The agent noticed only because it knew its own fix and screenshots could not exist on that branch, removed the stale worktree and re-ran; had it not noticed, the next integrate pass would have merged rejected code into main and UAT while the board read as a clean review. Fix so a wrong resolution is impossible rather than merely unlikely: when the item already records a branch, resolve to the worktree whose checked-out branch matches it and fail loudly on a mismatch; when several worktrees match the id, refuse and list them rather than picking one; and consider having finish print the resolved worktree and branch before it pushes so a human or agent can catch it. Note also that abandon and list share this resolver, so the same ambiguity can remove or report the wrong tree. Related: abandoned worktrees are evidently not always cleaned up, which is what supplies the second match.** [owner: claude] (done 2026-09-21, 884d400e2f9f16331626a6546800cd20dd3b8b1c)

The /design preview switcher in app/design/g124-upcoming-refine/G124Client.tsx now carries 8 controls (3 variants, 3 interval rules, a state toggle and a mode toggle) and overflows its max-w-[calc(100vw-16px)] container, so at 500px and below the rightmost chip is clipped off the right edge. It scrolls horizontally so every control is reachable, but the ACTIVE chip is the one clipped when the live interval rule is 'At a cluster', which is the default, so Kevin's first load shows the current selection half cut off. Raised from the G127 round-three review 2026-09-18, where it was the third of three findings; the other two (merged-divider hairline collapse, stale /design index entry) were fixed on feature-G127-round3-fix and that branch was merged. Worth solving generically rather than per preview, since any future round that adds a fourth dimension hits the same wall: options include wrapping to two rows at narrow widths, scrolling the active chip into view on mount, or grouping the secondary toggles behind a single control.** [owner: claude]
- [ ] **H87. Remove Yapily entirely (Kevin 2026-09-19): the Finexer migration plan always ended with removing the dormant Yapily integration and it never happened, 35 backend files under app/ still reference it including a router, a sync service, a yapily_accounts collection with consent filtering in the Safe-to-Spend hardening, and the account-pool logic, plus 3 frontend files and the YAPILY_APP_UUID, YAPILY_SECRET and YAPILY_BASE_URL variables in docs/ops/ENV.md and both environments; remove the router, services, collection handles, env vars and frontend references, migrate or drop any yapily_accounts documents on UAT and production with a backup first and Kevin's confirmation before the production drop, delete the Yapily entries from BANK_META, and update ENV.md, DEPLOY.md and the Finexer compliance answers wherever Yapily is named; do this after A-section item 1 above lands so the connect paths are settled first** [owner: claude] [priority: p2]
- [ ] **H88. Two board items currently have duplicate worktrees, which is the live condition H85 exists to make safe. Found 2026-09-21 by the H85 work: G89 has /root/worktrees/feature-G89-fold-in-approved-variant-b and feature-G89-story-first-reset with NO branch recorded on the board, and G94 has feature-G94-fold-in-approved-variant-c and feature-G94-story-first-reset while sitting in state rejected with feature-G94-fold-in-approved-variant-c recorded. G94 is the dangerous one: a session picking that rejection up and running finish today resolves through the pre-H85 glob-and-take-first, so which branch gets pushed and marked review depends on filesystem ordering, exactly the shape that pushed already-rejected code on 2026-09-18. H85 fixes the resolver so this refuses or resolves correctly, but the stale directories should still go, both to remove the hazard before H85 merges and because a worktree left behind after a merge is what supplies the second match in the first place. Work out which of the four are genuinely abandoned (check whether their branches are merged, and whether any session is live on them) and remove only those, with scripts/session.sh abandon or git worktree remove. Do not remove a worktree another session is working in. Worth also asking why they were never cleaned up, since H85's resolver cannot stop duplicates being created, only stop them being resolved wrongly.** [owner: claude]
- [ ] **H89. scripts/session.sh start aborts before it can explain an import failure. Flagged during H85's command-substitution audit 2026-09-21 and deliberately left alone there to keep a shared-tooling fix tightly scoped. cmd_start's 'import app' venv check around line 638 is a bare assignment at function level, which is a context where errexit DOES fire, so when the import fails the script exits immediately and the explanatory err block written just below it never prints. The session sees a bare non-zero exit with no indication that the worktree's venv is the problem, which is the opposite of what that code was written to do. This is the inverse of the H85 bug in the same file: there, errexit was suppressed inside a command substitution whose assignment status was tested, so a refusal did not propagate; here it propagates too eagerly and swallows the diagnosis. Worth checking the whole script for both shapes at once rather than case by case, and worth a test that drives cmd_start with a deliberately broken venv and asserts the explanation reaches the user.** [owner: claude]
- [ ] **H90. Backend tests run from a worktree write into the REAL wealth database, including documents for Kevin's own user id. Confirmed 2026-09-23 during the G148 review: two response_cache documents carry a shape key that exists only on the unmerged feature-G148 branch, written at 07:03 and 07:08, while wealth-api and wealth-worker both run from the shared tree on main and were restarted at 08:49, so neither could have written them. One is a debt_plan for kevin.maingi12@gmail.com at version 938, his current data version, day 2026-09-23, which on main's code (no shape check) is a valid cache hit and was therefore being served to him. Inspected read-only: it holds 7 cards with real card names, so it is a genuine compute rather than fixture data, and it expires at the next version bump, so the immediate harm is low and it has been left alone. The mechanism is the problem: backend/tests/conftest.py documents that this repo has no test database and that the first Mongo-touching test in a session writes to the real wealth DB. So every session that runs the backend suite, which session.sh finish does on every finish, can write derived documents for real users from unmerged code. Today it was a harmless cache entry; the same path could write a monkeypatched or half-migrated document under a real user_id and nothing would flag it. Give the suite its own database or an in-memory Mongo, or at minimum fail hard when a test would write to the configured production or UAT database. Note this also undermines the never-create-user-data rule every session brief carries, since a session can comply with it and still cause writes through the suite.** [owner: claude]
  - note (2026-09-23, claude): Second mechanism found 2026-09-23, and it is the one that wrote to Kevin's real uid rather than a fixture one. compute_today_items' persist=False does NOT suppress the engine's internal response_cache.aput. The G148 agent chose persist=False specifically because it is the documented non-mutating mode /today/cover-plan runs on every Settings load, and reasoned that made the call read-only; it does suppress item-lifecycle writes and celebration stamps, but the response-cache write still happens. So a session following the never-create-user-data rule, deliberately picking the read-only path, still wrote a document under Kevin's user id from unmerged code. The parameter's name and its docstring both imply more than they deliver, which is the actual trap: this was not carelessness, it was a correct-looking read of the API. Fix the naming and the docstring as part of this item, or give the function a genuinely read-only mode, so the next session that reasons the same way is right. The other write, at 07:03 under the fixture uid alloc-reservation-user, came from an ordinary full backend suite run and happens on main too, which is the conftest half of this item.
  - note (2026-09-23, claude): Correction to this item's framing, 2026-09-23, after reading backend/tests/conftest.py properly. I wrote that conftest documents the suite writing to the real wealth DB. What it actually guards is narrower: _mongo_cleanup_allowed at line 28 gates the CLEANUP fixture, the one that would WIPE cache and version collections, behind an explicit TEST_DB=1 opt-in or a MONGO_URI pointing at a database named or suffixed _test, neither set by default here. So the repo has thought about not destroying real data and has not thought about not creating it: nothing stops a test writing a document under any user id, and the 07:03 write under the fixture uid alloc-reservation-user is exactly that. The second write, 07:08 under Kevin's real uid, did not come from pytest at all; it came from an agent verification script calling compute_today_items(persist=False), which is the separate persist=False trap recorded in the note above. So there are two distinct holes, not one, and only the wiping case is currently defended. Also worth chasing: codex's G155 note claims a finish gate that ran 3,092 backend tests 'against an isolated disposable MongoDB'. If that is real and not just wording, the mechanism may already exist on their side and this item is about adopting it rather than inventing it; conftest.py on main contains no such thing, so find out what they actually ran before building anything.
